# Security invariants

State privacy intent separately from enforcement. Check reports proved, refuted
with a replayed counterexample, or unproved with a reason; passing finite tests
are not proofs.

```quarry
table privateNotes:
  patient: principal
  body: text 1..2000
  sealed: bool default false
  read <- .patient = viewer and .sealed = false
  insert <- .patient = viewer and .sealed = false
  online only
-- Spec: a patient may see only their own note; sealing may narrow availability.
-- Removing an accidental broad branch from the rule must not change this intent.
invariant privateNotes.read only .patient = viewer
invariant privateNotes.body read only .patient = viewer
invariant privateNotes.insert only .patient = viewer
query privateNote(note: privateNotes):
  return privateNotes[note]
mutation writePrivateNote(body: text 1..2000):
  return insert privateNotes { patient: viewer, body }
```

Run `snapback4 check` → `snapback4 test` → `snapback4 audit`. Check reports
invariant counts, five ordinary note classes and all likely-hole questions; `--verbose` retains every reason.
Answer the rule questions from the product specification before editing intent.
The restating advisory names each admitted role and its row conditions: for
example, a coach branch with no assignment check asks whether coaches should
see other coaches' athletes. For every role, answer which rows, which columns,
and which status/consent conditions the spec permits. Include derived values
and downloads: hiding a diagnosis but exposing its category can reveal it.
Write that answer as independent read intent; repair a rule that admits more.
A matching or weaker assertion may still be the right simple owner policy:
keep confirmed intent. The advisory never refuses it or changes its verdict.
Review `snapback/generated/security-invariants.q.draft`: each role gets a row
question and a question for every column, including roles absent from the rule.
No candidate is accepted automatically. Use table-read intent for a shared row audience; add
column-read intent for distinct privacy boundaries. Assess every role and field
without automatically creating one assertion per question. For each role, identify
a positive grant from the spec or a necessary described workflow before retaining
access to another person's private data. Confirm intentionally shared fields
explicitly; an omitted denial is not a product grant.
Run check, test and audit again after confirming intent;
a refutation means fix the enforcement or the independently verified spec,
without widening intent just to make the result green.

Test synthesizes two users per discovered role/seat and related ownership rows
without fixture files, including password-only apps. These private in-memory
fixtures never open dev doors. Every failure labels its initial fixture origin:
`generated-fixture-trace` means an authored observation from a validated generated
initial state, not proof that app operations created that state. Author-supplied
fixtures retain `authored-fixture-trace`. Both fail acceptance on actual observed
violations; seed-only/synthetic table probes remain non-failing supplemental evidence.
Fixture-free tests report that no authored business cases ran. Finite passing
campaigns never prove security. Check bounds concrete exploration to 512 call/world
reservations and 1500 ms; exhaustion stays unproved and points to the full test.
`audit --json` retains all column audiences, grant writers, exclusions and uncovered
role/row/effect exposures. Audit returns the permission map with bounded check
evidence; run `test` for the full generated campaign. `audit --all` shows every
reason, without increasing the search. Declaring one effect does not cover the others.

## Reference

`invariant table.column read only P` protects a column value, derived values and value-dependent counts/existence/refusals. Mutation results, implicit final writes and explicit returned row objects are observers too. A query projection alone is not privacy: use column read rules to protect every operation. An authored projection excluding the column does not by itself disclose that value; filtering, ordering or copying it can. Audit JSON includes `column_audiences` (effective row rule plus private-owner gate). Unknown derived and opaque paths remain unproved.

`invariant table.read only P` protects read intent; insert, update and delete
name authority. `invariant table.column write only P` covers writes to a
column (insert, inserted insert-if-absent, update and both upsert arms).
A null creation or the declared `false` approval default is initialization, not an approval write. Mandatory creation presence needs whole-row insert/write or always intent; non-null/non-default creation still requires P. Tests judge each executed write in a committed mutation, including intermediate writes. `invariant table.column update only P` checks
only edits, retaining old-and-next-image semantics; use it when creation is permitted
to a different role (for example counter staff creating a ticket price). `invariant table always P` states row integrity.
An update policy over `.field` constrains **both old and next images**.
A policy saying status must not be override therefore also restricts editing
a row that was already override, even when changing it away from that status.
For the role authority of one named mutation, use operation-specific intent
(`invariant table.operation only P`) instead. An operator `provision` result excludes only its own executed roster write from authored read intent; later queries and other results remain checked. General next-image-only and
cross-operation transition assertions still need a separate reduction.
Predicates use the existing rule vocabulary: `.field`, `viewer`, `exists`,
comparisons, `and`, `or`, `not`. For a role write use the complete roster probe,
for example `invariant members.role write only exists roles[viewer, role = 'owner']`
in a schema declaring those tables. A declaration never grants authority. Whole-row read intent also protects logical
existence: when occupancy totals are intentionally public but roster identities
are private, declare column read intent for those identities instead. Include
a service seat explicitly when the spec permits it; seats are ordinary checked
viewers, never implicitly exempt.

The implemented proof fragment exhaustively checks independent three-valued
rule predicates. A failed implication is unproved until an engine replay
establishes a counterexample. A schema-valid seeded snapshot is not a claim
that an application operation sequence reaches that state. Required-column
non-null assertions have a separate validation theorem. Read/count/sum/existence obligations are reported separately. A maintained aggregate with bare privacy keys can prove its contributor audience; a broader aggregate can yield a paired present/absent counterexample. Exact server `not before`/`expire at` bounds can prove a temporal read component independently of physical sweeps. General transitions and unsupported expressions remain unproved.
Read-through captures/live parents, complex derived aggregates, raw rule probes,
owned-field update bypasses, cascade/set-null and opaque jobs/workers need
separate reductions. An unresolved path blocks the whole declaration's proof.

Server delivery authorizes against the current server snapshot. A disconnected
device can retain an earlier snapshot until it receives a revocation; even
`until-revoked` cannot know a later server role. Synced current-role intent is
therefore unproved for device access. Retained history follows its leave policy.
These reports exclude physical timing and resource/meter side channels and
explicitly exclude the trusted system-principal boundary. Service seats remain
inside the ordinary-viewer check; there is no implicit seat exemption.

## Current roles and derived grants

A membership or assignment created while a user is an inspector can survive
their demotion. Conjoin the **current reader's role** with the assignment
branch, or revoke every derived grant transactionally whenever the role changes.
A grant writer's role does not establish the recipient's current authority.
With independent current-role intent, grant-lifetime advice points at this pattern; the argument-grant advice identifies
arguments written into reader-grant columns: validate the chosen principal's
role and relationship, even when the caller is privileged.
Global admin authority must come from a trusted operator or existing admin;
a group-creation `created` probe must remain scoped to that group everywhere.
`N_PRIVILEGE_ESCALATION` identifies role assignments trusted by later rules without independent delegation. Open enrollment may be intentional: compare each note with the specification. See `guide staging` for operator first-admin setup and `guide sharing` for parent authority.

## Required evidence and deadlines

If the spec requires a photo, use a required `image <=5000000` column or
`[image <=5000000] 1..4`; `?` permits omission and `[image] <=4` permits zero.
Uploads are verified media and asset reads inherit row and private-field rules.
Optional-evidence advice asks the builder to check intentional optionality.
Text `min..max` counts non-whitespace Unicode characters toward the minimum;
the maximum counts all characters. Stored text is not trimmed or rewritten.
A sweep changes state eventually: compare the deadline with server `now` in
the mutation too if writes must stop exactly at that instant.

## Existence observations

A lookup that says NOT_FOUND on absence followed by a denial saying FORBIDDEN
can reveal existence to callers admitted by a broader read rule. Use the same
code **and message** for hidden/absent/denied cases when existence is private,
or tighten the table read rule. Program `exists` is caller-filtered; probes
inside table rules inspect raw rows and can disclose predicates indirectly.
Inspect both paths in `audit`; a payload-only read check is insufficient.

## When an observer remains unproved

The report names the edge that lacks a reduction. Preserve the specification
while changing its enforcement; weakening an invariant to match a leaking
rule does not repair privacy.

- Maintained counts/sums: retain privacy fields as ordinary bare group keys
  and protect the aggregate with the same audience predicate. Recorded
  reference paths, calendar keys, derived values, private columns and temporal
  contributors need their own reduction; their names are not source fields.
- Owner fields: declare column-write intent too. `written by` authorizes
  patches even when the ordinary update rule denies. To prove a particular
  mutation's role authority, use a dominating current-role requirement and
  operation-specific intent over a role table the mutation cannot change.
  This does not prove every owner-field or old/next-image path.
- Raw rule probes: inspect the observing table and its writers. A protected
  target's read rule does not protect predicates probed from another rule.
  Apply the intended audience at that observer, or remove the raw dependency;
  the report retains the unsupported edge until it can establish implication.
- Cascade/set-null: automatic child changes bypass child write rules. An
  explicit authorized child mutation with `restrict` removes that automatic
  writer, but parent refusal/existence and transitive references remain
  separate observations. Read-through/live-parent authority requires a separate reduction.
- Jobs/native workers/actions: the audit names the trusted or opaque writer.
  An ordinary mutation with stable role requirements can prove that mutation's
  authority; arbitrary native/worker output cannot acquire a proof from a
  caller's role or a passing finite test.
- Schedules: use a stored `not before`/`expire at` lifetime for exact read
  boundaries, or compare server `now` in the write itself. A scheduled status
  update leaves the old status visible between deadline and sweep.

Default `test` challenges pure-schema applications through a private production
`serve`: unauthenticated calls, forged persona headers and development doors.
The host uses no pre-existing developer credentials or development mode. Applications needing
native/worker artifacts are explicitly not covered by that schema-only host.
Focused fixtures also enable short ordered mutation histories followed by
ordinary reads; traces record actual calls and current policy evaluation.
Named personas/service seats and enum argument combinations are enumerated
within reported limits. Asset challenges use real foreign-upload and verified
non-image ledgers; video arguments and unvisited histories remain named. Complete fixture stores preserve image/file ledgers, captures, read-through deadlines, maintained views and expiry. Histories include fixture principals and reference-row candidates, under a reported work cap. Neither
these finite campaigns nor an offline device's retained snapshot establishes
current-server revocation at a disconnected access.

# What Snapback 4 Does Not Do

Longer than the doing-list on purpose. Moving something off it means writing one
line naming what it unblocks and taking something off the doing-list in the same
change. Nothing comes off during slice one (LLP 3000 §3: the clock is four weeks).

## The bars

The bakeoff gates in [LLP 3000 §3](../llp/3000-snapback4.explainer.md) are the
bars: the loop bar, hub acquisition within 2× the measured floor, client bytes
under the competitor-anchored budgets, the six scenarios green in the simulator
on the hub fixture. Everything not required to pass them does not exist yet.

## Not building

- **A second authoring language.** Quarry, one spelling, copied from Snapback 2 (LLP 1004, LLP 1007 §4b). TypeScript is for seeds, journeys, effect bodies and the client.
- **Byte- and step-priced certification.** No closure, heap, output or step ceilings at `check`; the certificate certifies shape, uniformity, completeness class and prediction eligibility. Resources are metered at runtime (LLP 2000.000 §6).
- **A device per-query result cache.** No range receipts, halos, absence evidence, coverage spans, extent facts, held directories, fact integrity or swap journals. The client replicates declared partitions with one watermark each (LLP 2000.000 §7). The server's narrow Postgres revision-equality cache and explicitly clock-qualified G6 read grant are the exceptions below; cross-revision promotion remains excluded.
- **Engine-minted ids for client inserts, grants, ID slices.** Ids are client-minted, time-ordered, 128-bit; the engine mints for its own writes.
- **The `drain` protocol, follower-tab inbox rows, `defineItems`, `withHistory`, `mutationTarget`, `receipt.source`, `local.caughtUp`, `partial` reason codes.** The card (`packages/snapback4/src/contract.ts`) is the whole client vocabulary; `why` carries the rest.
- **`retain` as a receipt rule.** `until-revoked` and `delivered-history` survive as the client's leave policy on a scope tombstone.
- **JavaScript in the data plane.** Queries and mutations run in the Rust interpreter inside the SQLite transaction, and in the same interpreter on the client (host decided at week 3 by measurement, LLP 2000.000 §9). A *job* may be the app's own Rust, hosted as wasm through the interpreter's operations under the job's meter (LLP 3000.005, the Moves line below); a query or mutation may not.
- **An embedded JS engine for effects.** The data plane never calls out. `emit` writes the effect durably in the mutation's commit and an external worker holding a service token claims, performs and settles it over the wire (`snapback4/worker`); the four reference effects run on the builder's Node (LLP 2000.000 §10, decided 2026-09-13).
- **Committed generated code; an auth mode; a supervised bundler.** As Snapback 2 (`rules/NOT-DOING.md`): types by inference, personas not modes, `SNAPBACK_URL` is the client contract.
- **A TypeScript twin of the interpreter.** Retired by the author's ruling of 2026-09-13 (LLP 3000 §7): one implementation, in Rust, on every host — the core's SQLite under the native module, memory under wasm on the web with the host persisting each commit's changes. The web pays 312,924 B gzip before the diet where the twin's tier paid 16,842; the conformance obligation between two interpreters ends. Expo Go runs the online tier.
- **More than one hosted target, an admin console, media transcoding or thumbnails, push delivery itself (a worker's), cron syntax beyond `every N at HH:MM`, vector or relevance-ranked search (word-prefix search is in: `words(..)` and `matching`), federation, multi-region, device-to-device sync, at-rest encryption claims.** After the clock, on evidence.
- **General observability beyond `why`, `trace`, `logs`, `data`, `sql`, server operation latency, and bounded client subscription inspection.** The `sql` verb is read-only inspection of the developer's own store (LLP 3000.007 §8.8), never an application surface. Separately declared application SQL reads are admitted for increment D (LLP 3000.009 §6.2, §14.4; the 2026-09-16 Moves line below), not implemented by this inspection verb.
- **A guide corpus.** The compiler's facts, one language card and keyword cards, as Snapback 2; no freshness dates. Every card's Quarry is compiled by the suite and the language card is run, so a card cannot promise a form the parser refuses.

- **Partial union delivery, two group audiences, a public branch.** A union syncs principal-column branches and at most one group branch, each a whole disjunct of the read rule (LLP 3000.036.000.000); a branch with an extra condition, a second group or a `public` branch is refused with the rewrite, never partially delivered; service grants add no device audience (LLP 3000.007 §20.6). A union does not combine with delivered history, read-through children, synced or row-keyed views, owner keys, timelines, private field owners, acquisition windows or direct/inherited expiry on content or membership scopes.
- **Exact horizon maintenance.** The horizon bounds acquisition (a snapshot, a bootstrap); the log never evicts or back-fills to hold a device's group at exactly N rows. A local read past the acquired horizon by key says `complete: false`.

- **Clock expressions in rules.** A read rule compares with `=` and `!=` only; `.expiresAt > now` remains a mutation `require`. Stored lifetimes use `not before .col` for server-controlled opening and `expire at .col` for expiry. The engine publishes opening commits; devices receive no unopened payload. The supported compositions and explicit exclusions are in LLP 3000.036.003.
- **A jobs framework.** No priorities, worker pools or dead letters: one FIFO of runs, one chunk at a time, a refused chunk parked with its refusal. Actions, effects and scheduled actions carry per-operation retries, deadlines and cancellation, and a record per external step (LLP 3000.009 §5.2; the Moves line below). Missed period boundaries while a server is down fire once on return, not once per missed period.
- **Asset processing.** No transcoding, thumbnails, rungs or HEIC: the bytes are served as uploaded; JPEG, PNG, WebP and MP4 only; image dimensions and optional MP4 duration read from the header; MP4 dimensions are not extracted.
- **Search beyond word prefixes.** One tokenizer (NFC, lowercase, alphanumeric words), one index shape (`scope, words(cols), order.., id`), one read (`matching`): every term a prefix of some word, the first term's words merged as lanes, bounded by the meter in a uniform scope, at most 32 words in an admitted non-uniform scope. No stemming, no ranking by relevance, no phrase or mid-word matching, no per-language folding, no intersection index for the second term (it filters the page).
- **An identity provider.** Guests, password accounts, service seats, and sessions a service mints for accounts the application authenticated itself. No phone, OAuth, magic links or MFA in the engine: an application's worker verifies those and mints the session.
- **No multi-server presence, no presence history or last-seen storage, no broadcast event stream, no connection-bound rows.** `connected[p]` stays server-wide, counting held polls and their 35 s grace; a live query that may read it re-runs in the background about every 5 seconds to stay current (LLP 3000.018). Typing and viewing use short-lived, authorized ephemeral tables in one server process (LLP 3000.017); they never sync or decide durable invariants. A device does not know presence and says so.

## Process not doing

- **2026-09-28 — ordinary mixed search** (LLP 3000.028 §3.3, lane OS round 1).
  Admit the SDK's bounded merge of current server rows and certified Left images
  for the proved single time-ordered word page. This removes application-owned
  online/held selection for that shape. One server call per requested page;
  recomputation pays again from consumed positions. The coordinator shares the
  existing device read allowance. No durable search session, extra synced rows,
  new authority or additional allowance is admitted.

- **2026-09-28 — inferred current-scope word search** (LLP 3000.028 §3.3,
  lane OS). A single bare ordinary word page may derive the exact non-expiring
  membership audience for the existing compact server plan. This removes the
  requirement to repeat that audience predicate in this narrow server query.
  The compiler supplies a scoped words index when absent; it is an ordinary
  schema index, maintained on writes and on devices, with no extra synced rows
  or new authority. The device keeps its original bare scan. Round 0 left ordered composition
  and combined accounting outside its independent subset; round 1 above admits
  that bounded SDK composition. Durable search sessions remain excluded.
- **2026-10-12 — union sync audiences** (LLP 3000.036.000.000, workstream UNION, two blind review rounds). A table syncs to the union of principal-column audiences and one group audience; a device holds a row while any branch admits its viewer. What it unblocks: owner-or-team, buyer-or-seller and owner-or-staff tables offline for every reader, the shape three app farms asked for. Taken off: "one `sync` audience per table". Partial delivery, two groups and a public branch stay excluded (above).
- **2026-09-28 — explicit owner-key coverage** (LLP 3000.028 §3.4, Lane OK commission). A public window may opt into a compiler-owned, horizon-free principal key partition to answer ordinary own-key existence offline. Taken off: the exclusion of narrower platform key distribution beside one public window. General union audiences, full own-row replication and per-query absence caches remain excluded. The projection uses existing metered maintains, adoption and whole-partition publication; existing windows do not expand.
- **2026-09-26 — a live query keeps presence current** (LLP 3000.018). A 0.4.2 probe found an observed `connected[p]` reporting `fresh: true` while stale for over a minute, and a comparative builder polled around it. What it unblocks: online marks and presence-aware lists that stay current. Taken off: "a presence stream" as an exclusion for live queries; a presence API, last-seen storage and multi-server presence stay out. Under the author's standing authorization for reworks that make queries excellent (2026-09-25).

- **No drift registries, inventories, line-pinned diagnostics registries, claim digests or frozen-record gates.** Generated projections are built, not committed.
- **No gate bound to an exact commit; no push gated on a battery.** Five blocking checks in sixty seconds, run before push; everything slower on a schedule with bounded tickets.
- **No ceiling raised in slice one.** Budgets are anchored to a competitor's measured number and recorded once in the journal.
- **No standing panel** except the two blind reviews LLP 3000 decision 4 names (partition inversion; the meter's cut on non-uniform scans). The author explicitly commissioned the one-off LLP 3000.008 panel and disjoint Astra implementation lanes on 2026-09-14; its bounded disposition is recorded in that umbrella and LLP 3000 §7.
- **No new LLP number** without first extending LLP 3000 or LLP 2000.000; no spec without running code (`rules/RULES.md`: only implemented specs bind).
- **No second session on the core** during slice one; lanes take the periphery.
- **No test matrix as the primary harness.** The simulator is; real-browser and device witnesses cover only what it cannot reach.
- **No files over 1,500 lines**, including ones copied from Snapback 2. Split on
  the way in. The one exception is the package's published documents
  (`packages/snapback4/README.md` and its linked references): the rule exists to
  keep a *source* file reviewable, and the 0.2.25 calibration measured the cost of
  applying it to a document — splitting the README was that run's single largest
  scoring loss, because the builder then had four places to look instead of one
  (LLP 3000.007 §20.19). A published document is bounded by whether a builder can
  find one way in, not by a line count; the census that enforces this rule covers
  source, and says so.

## Deliberately worse

- No compatibility with Snapback 1 or Snapback 2 apps, packages or on-disk formats.
- No API stability before the clock; every publish is `0.0.x`.
- Sparse prose documentation; the diagnostics and the recipes carry the load.
- One hosted target, one platform binary at a time. SQLite remains the default store; Postgres deploy/serve admits the initial Unix-socket HTTP profile under LLP 3000.009.001 (Moves, 2026-10-20; `guide release-json`). Provider HA, full parity and publication remain outside that profile.

## Moves

- **2026-10-24 — a bounded server read grant moves onto the doing-list** (author's G6 commission, LLP 3000.009.001.000.002 §4.2). It unblocks zero-flight Postgres hits after G5's measured validation CPU limit. Taken off the doing-list: mandatory per-hit database validation for the explicitly qualified, single-owner Linux deployment. Unqualified clocks/failover and lost/uncertain epochs use G5; device result caches and cross-revision promotion remain excluded.

- **2026-10-24 — Postgres server revision-equality read reuse** (LLP
  3000.009.001.000.002 §4/§6 G5). This removes repeated interpretation and
  data decoding for eligible pure queries at an unchanged durable revision.
  Taken off: the blanket server result-cache exclusion. Every reuse still
  validates fresh authority, revision and code in one database statement;
  SQLite stays on ordinary reads because it has no complete revision stream.
  Device caches, dependency promotion, retained authority and zero-flight
  grants stay excluded. Operator-local counters add no HTTP or authoring API.

- **2026-10-20 — Snapback 4 on Postgres moves onto the doing-list** (author: "commit and push these things, then do your \"what i'd do next\" things, then get this working on postgres and measure it"; LLP 3000.009.001 and its children). Unblocks: the store-port extraction on SQLite (P0, including the `Committer` seam with the serial batch); the Postgres adapters for every durable-state family and `serve`/`deploy`/`doctor` over Postgres (P1), with store-held fencing, the declared HA mode and `BlobStore`; the optimistic committer, commissioned on 2026-10-19 and built after its execution stage is sized; the scale program of LLP 3000.009.001.002 with its raw-Postgres, Supabase and Convex controls on fleet machines; and a research note comparing replicated SQLite with Postgres for applications that outgrow one SQLite file. Taken off in the same change: the evaluation-only framing of the Postgres adapter. Stands: no publication or managed-service deployment authority, no lowering of Quarry to SQL, no SQL writes to managed data, one core owner. Several serving processes (P2), log retention, live graduation (LLP 3000.009.001.001) and the async-safe external anchor each wait for their own Moves line.
# Rules

**Snapback 2 optimizes the edit-to-verified loop — the agent's building an app on it, and ours building it.**

Speed is the constraint. Everything below exists to keep the edit-to-verified loop short
and to stop this repository accreting apparatus faster than anyone can remove it. These
rules bind `snapback2/` and the LLPs numbered 1000 and up; Snapback 1 is baselined, not
exempt, and its numbers may only fall.

Each rule is **[check]** (enforced by `caps`) or **[review]** (enforced by a human).

## Budgets — every budget is a trade, not a limit

- **5 blocking checks, 60s total.** A sixth requires deleting one, in the same PR. **[check]**
- **59 documents in the working set** (`llp/current/`). Today's count. A 60th requires
  archiving one — which is just removing the link. Snapback 2 aims to leave this at 15;
  Snapback 1's links are archived at promotion. **[check]**
- **12 documents in the foundation** (`llp/foundation/`). **[check]**
- **This file: 700 words.** If it grows, something becomes a check or stops being a rule. **[check]**
- **1,500 lines per source file, baseline 204.** Generated files exempt — and generated
  files are built, not committed; so are benchmark run records under `benchmarks/runs/`,
  which are evidence, not source. The baseline is Snapback 1's oversize count on the day
  the rule landed; it may not grow, and `snapback2/` contributes zero to it. **[check]**

## Blocking checks

`caps` · `ref-check` · `snapback2/scripts/test.sh` · `snapback2/scripts/measure.mjs --check` · `check-inventories`

## Loop shape

- **Every check reports all failures in one run.** Fail-fast is banned. **[check]**
- **Nothing blocks on anything slower than 60 seconds.** Slow verification runs on a
  schedule, attributed to the breaking commit, and files a bounded set of issues. **[review]**
- **Fix loops get 3 rounds.** Then stop: escalate to a human or descope. **[review]**

## Time budgets

Tracked every run of `snapback2/scripts/measure.mjs` (appends `snapback2/metrics/loop.jsonl`).
A regression is a P0 with a name on it.

| | budget |
|---|---|
| Incremental rebuild after a one-line edit | 15s |
| Test what you just changed | 30s |
| Blocking gate suite | 60s |
| Full build, warm cache | 5 min |
| **`snapback2 check` on a program (the agent's product latency)** | **1s** |
| **`snapback2 doctor` boot-to-ready** | **0.5s** |

## Scope

- **`NOT-DOING.md` is binding.** Moving something onto the doing-list means writing why
  and taking something off. **[review]**
- **Delete; don't deprecate.** No compat shims or migration paths before promotion. **[review]**
- **Nothing is compiled, transpiled, or generated at runtime that could be built ahead of
  time.** **[review]**
- **A spec needs an implementer and a date, or it isn't written.** **[review]**
- **Only implemented specs bind.** A document with no running code behind it is a
  proposal, and a proposal cannot block a PR or justify a check. **[review]**

## Agents

- **Agents remove apparatus freely and add none.** An agent PR that adds a check, script,
  registry, config file, or design doc needs explicit human approval saying so. **[review]**
- **2026-09-06 session exception:** the user delegated the search implementation
  decisions; one `snapback2-core/build.rs` may generate pinned Unicode tables at
  build time, alongside the existing package build. No new gate, registry,
  runtime generator, or Unicode dependency is authorized by this exception. **[review]**
- **Snapback 2 LLPs are numbered 1000 and up**; `llp-create` must be told. **[review]**
- **Snapback 4 LLPs are numbered 3000 and up** (`snapback4/`, LLP 3000); it adopts these budgets
  and loop shape as its own and keeps its scope in `snapback4/NOT-DOING.md`. **[review]**

## Shared machines

- Warm, named lanes, one per stream of work. Never clone fresh; reuse the worktree.
- **Never `git stash`.** Use a temporary commit.
- Kill only PIDs you recorded at launch. Never by name or pattern.

The four recovery witnesses fail before and pass after, including both cascade
sites and the mixed-deadline case; each checks durable receipts, contiguous
head and serial-equivalent state. The pipeline module now shares the SQLite
feature gate with program batching. Device and browser configurations fail
before with unresolved server-only imports and build afterward. Final core
and device builds without default features, native device, and browser wasm
all pass; wasm is 738,071 B gzip under the 738,490 B budget.

Postgres depth two drains available FIFO work and dispatches at the first empty
mailbox, within the existing target, 32-member cap and 1 ms collection bound.
It does not wait for predicted clients still held by the other writer. Depth
one and SQLite retain their coalescing policy, and idle arrivals never wait.
The policy witness fails before and passes after. RFC §8.1 documents the rule.

Two reversed-order loopback repetitions at 32 closed-loop clients record depth
1 → 2: W4 4,434 → 4,551 writes/s, 7.081 → 6.817 ms; W5-exact 4,531 → 4,803,
6.917 → 6.579 ms; W5-mix 4,602 → 4,811, 6.858 → 6.499 ms. Mean N falls from
30.48/30.48/31.22 to 15.24/15.42/15.33. Outstanding-batch dispatch histograms
(1, 2) are W4 (84, 0) → (4, 164), exact (84, 0) → (2, 164), mix
(82, 0) → (3, 164). All counters reconcile. Load is about 84 on 96 available
CPUs; these short local relative samples establish overlap, without an S1 or
general speedup claim.

Final full core: 1,278/0/480 (passed/failed/ignored); binary: 608/0/57;
focused G7.2: 33/0; core/HTTP RT0: 4/0 and 9/0. The complete Postgres script
records 847/2: only the on-wire-overlap and TCP_NODELAY witnesses explicitly
refuse socket-only qualification. The inherited evaluator 40001 does not occur.
Public serving passes, including release evolution, recovery, ownership, wire
parity and scale recounts. Both private clusters independently confirm status
3 with no pidfile. Final reference and PID audit results are in the report.
Raw evidence stays outside Git; no push, stash or Git configuration change occurs.

Final ref-check reports 569 LLPs and 11,984 references, zero errors or unchecked
references. All 8,732 continuation PID/birth instances, the prior run's 317,
and 537 public-script PID records are absent; the monitor has exited. PIDs left:
none. Minimum free disk is 123.83 GiB, final audit 128.77 GiB; the 30 GiB guard
never fires.
### 2.1 First membership and developer refusal detail

Lane MP, commissioned 2026-09-28, reproduces calibration 24's first member
insert: its insert rule accepts `created conversations[.conversation]`, but
its read rule requires an already existing membership. It therefore fails
before allocating an ordinal. `check` now reports `N_SEQUENCE_READ` at the
sequence column with this exact cause and the manual receipt allocator.
This is a note, so existing programs retain their compilation and behavior.
Round 1 limits the categorical note to a self-table witness whose selected
index maps the sequence-parent column to that same row's parent term.
Round 2 also accepts a residual equality on that exact parent column and term,
including a literal-key witness without `viewer`; it requires no existing
member to belong to the viewer, only to the same parent group. Merely
finding `.parent` elsewhere in the key proves nothing: `access = .parent`
can find a witness in another parent group and admit its first insertion.
Unproved forms receive no categorical note. The rewrite names the declared
sequence column and its parent; it does not invent a message table or ordinal
column in a members-only schema. The guide holds the shared-counter recipe.

**2026-10-13 — FLAKE shutdown witness.** The real refusal writer accepts an
internal clock/output seam: production supplies `Instant::now` and stderr;
the test freezes only console aggregation time and captures its output. The
same `serve_requests` stopping branch must flush eight unexpired buffered
records while sender clones remain alive, then release the evidence lock.
The sink-drop completion signal joins actual writer cleanup before inspecting
that lock: the foreground return alone may precede it when its bounded shutdown
wait expires under CPU contention.
Signal-process tests still prove SIGINT/SIGTERM shutdown with held polls and
workers and preserve all eight records; their console count may span windows
when CPU scheduling stretches the burst. The exact one-second window is tested
with logical timestamps, without an eight-request 700 ms throughput assertion.

A provably fresh counter contains no hidden history, so allocating its first
ordinal would disclose nothing. Neither `created parent` nor an empty live
child group proves that fact in the current model. An ordinary reference
(including a sequence parent) is not a lifetime-enforcing foreign key. The
counter is shared across participating tables, survives deleted children and
parent identities, and can have history before this parent insertion. Even
an empty physical group may therefore reveal past allocations. Testing the
hidden counter for zero and allowing only that case would itself be a probe.
This lane does not add such an exception or reset a counter. Preserve the
membership audience with the guide's manual parent field, initialized on the
new parent and updated atomically by every sender; alternatively an explicitly
owner-only membership model may use its own read authority. That alternative
changes the roster audience and is not an automatic repair.

The exact refusing clause is developer evidence, separate from `Refusal`.
A host must opt in to a native-only, per-execution in-memory collector. The
collector records schema clause, table, execution site and generation, never
arguments, row images, row keys, credentials or hidden counts. It does not
perform reads, reevaluate rules, change admission, consume/refund meters, or
change receipt serialization. Authorization-budget exhaustion records that
cause, not a guessed false rule. The recorded phase is the authorization actually
executing: capture exhaustion maps to the child's `read through` declaration,
not its unevaluated insert rule. Query readers retain their source map together
with the checked-out engine generation across adoption.

Only `snapback4 dev` opts in. After releasing the engine lock and sending the
response, the host hands each response record to a bounded nonblocking writer queue.
The shared JSON response layer covers every refusal envelope, including asset
authorization refusals (HTTP 404, E_ASSET_UNKNOWN), receipt replays and paths
with no collector. Every record carries operation, code, an instance-scoped
request ID or job ID, wall-clock Unix time in milliseconds, and a shared
sequence. Without an evaluated cause it says `cause not instrumented`; it
never leaves a previous request's cause standing for this refusal. A filtered
read cannot supply the cause of a later terminal refusal in the same request.

**Supervisor scope ruling, round 3 (2026-09-28):** `why recent` records
requests or job steps that the platform refused. Visibility filtering in sync
and reads is successful product behavior, not a refusal, and is not recorded;
recording every filtered sync would flood this bounded stream. Its scope text
names refusals of mutations, queries, asset requests and jobs, and directs
visibility questions to `why TABLE` (read rules and sync residency) and
`guide rules`. Operation, request/job identity and time distinguish older
evidence from a later successful sync/read. This supersedes round 2's
FILTERED_READ records. The read collector still observes evaluated verdicts
and allowance exhaustion; successful responses with a nonterminal filtering
cause do not enter the stream. **DX round 0 decision (2026-09-29, calibration #26):** ordinary empty answers
are not refusals. Superseding the round-3 fallback, unknown empty/null query
answers (including fields in composed results) are neither printed nor recorded
by default. They do not construct records, advance the evidence watermark, enqueue
work, format console lines or rewrite the refusal store. `dev --trace-empty`
opts into EMPTY_READ records in `why recent` only, never console notices. The
stream identity records `traceEmpty`; the guide/why EMPTY_READ card supplies
rule, coverage and horizon investigation commands and states that this evidence
does not prove denial. No caller bytes change. Real refusal envelopes, including
an application-authored EMPTY_READ code, keep their existing evidence path.
Successful visibility filtering remains excluded even with the opt-in.
### Source diagnostics without an existence oracle

Extend LLP 3000.036.002 §15.2's N_LOOKUP_FILTERED advice to required reads
and reads of written tables. A later write and an asserted `! else CODE` do
not prove read authority. Existing key/flow proofs still suppress advice,
including prior successful exact reads and deliberately public projections.
One note names the source lookup, authored hiding rule, declaration and the
null/custom-refusal consequence; duplicate N_LOOKUP_HIDDEN notes at that
site are avoided (the more specific N_LOOKUP_HIDDEN takes precedence). This is missing-proof advice, not a claim every caller
is refused. Program `exists` and rule-witness `exists` keep distinct semantics.

For private tables with secondary unique keys, source advice explains that
an unreadable conflicting row uses generic E_RULE (or an undisclosed write
target miss), even when the unique declaration has `else CODE`. The named
constraint applies only when the conflict can be disclosed. Do not return
the declared code only on hidden conflicts: that would violate LLP 3000 §7
and LLP 3000.036.002 §15.2. Public E_RULE repair text, if changed, is identical
for ordinary rule rejection and hidden collision. No row, key, actual hiding
clause or hidden-dependent meter enters the public refusal. Rewrites propose
a minimal readable key projection or operator-mediated ownership/claim flow,
never broader disclosure of private rows. Paired hidden/missing lookup tests assert equality of public diagnostics and
logical meters. Hidden/ordinary write-refusal tests compare public refusals
and receipts; write replies expose no meter.

//! Production refusals and individual dispositions share the same derived rule.
// @ref LLP 3000.036.004.000#5-derived-campaigns-and-production-deployment — authored refutations cannot be disposed of.
use super::*;
struct Scratch(std::path::PathBuf);impl Drop for Scratch{fn drop(&mut self){let _=std::fs::remove_dir_all(&self.0);}}
#[test]fn production_derived_fix_has_a_working_rule_bound_decision_path(){
    let dir=std::env::temp_dir().join(format!("sb4-sec9-deploy-{}",std::process::id()));let _scratch=Scratch(dir.clone());
    std::fs::create_dir_all(dir.join("snapback")).unwrap();let project=Project{root:dir};
    let source="table docs:\n  owner: principal\n  cell: text ?\n  read <- allow\n  insert <- allow\n  update (old, next) <- old.cell = next.cell or viewer = 'service:desk'\n  online only\nmutation create(owner: principal, cell: text ?):\n  return insert docs { owner, cell }\n";
    std::fs::write(project.schema_dir().join("schema.q"),source).unwrap();let sources=project.sources().unwrap();
    let bound=snapback4_lang::compile(&sources);assert!(bound.diagnostics.is_empty());let options=Options{positionals:Vec::new(),flags:BTreeMap::new(),json:false};
    assert!(enforce_release(release_loop(&project,&bound,&sources,None,&options).unwrap().as_ref(),&options).unwrap_err().contains("E_SECURITY_REVIEW"));
    let report=report(&project,&bound,&sources,&options);
    let decisions=super::super::security_reviews::review(&project,&sources,&report.notes,None).unwrap();
    let mut reviewed=0;
    for decision in decisions.iter().filter(|d|matches!(d.code.as_str(),"N_CREATE_EDIT_ASYMMETRY"|"N_DERIVED_WRITE_AUTHORITY")){
        super::super::security_reviews::review(&project,&sources,&report.notes,Some((&decision.id,"The specification deliberately gives creators broader authority"))).unwrap();reviewed+=1;
    }
    assert!(reviewed>=2,"static and replayed decisions must exist");
    let notice=release_loop(&project,&bound,&sources,None,&options).unwrap().unwrap();
    assert!(!notice.contains("  FIX "),"acknowledged candidates must not remain outstanding FIX: {notice}");
    let changed_source=source.replace("'service:desk'","'service:other'");
    std::fs::write(project.schema_dir().join("schema.q"),&changed_source).unwrap();let changed=project.sources().unwrap();let changed_bound=snapback4_lang::compile(&changed);
    assert!(enforce_release(release_loop(&project,&changed_bound,&changed,None,&options).unwrap().as_ref(),&options).is_err(),"a changed rule needs a new decision");
}
//! Candidate contracts from authored change guards and inspected approval data.
// @ref LLP 3000.036.004.000#5-derived-campaigns-and-production-deployment — inferred candidates require ordinary replay, never a proof claim.
use super::*;

// A complex operand may inspect positive evidence before producing null.
// Only a plain row-field access can establish a pure absence precondition.
fn plain_row(expression:&ast::Expr)->bool {match expression {
    ast::Expr::Name{..}|ast::Expr::Focal{..}=>true,
    ast::Expr::Assert{value,..}=>plain_row(value),
    ast::Expr::Read(read)=>read.computed.is_empty()&&read.seeks.is_empty()&&read.filter.is_none()&&read.range.is_none()&&read.matching.is_none()
        &&read.keys.iter().all(|key|matches!(key.value,ast::Expr::Name{..}|ast::Expr::Literal{..})),
    _=>false,
}}
fn plain_field(expression:&ast::Expr)->bool {
    matches!(expression,ast::Expr::Member{base,..} if plain_row(base))
}

fn omit_preconditions(expression:&mut ast::Expr,negated:bool){
    match expression {
        ast::Expr::Unary{op,value,..} if op=="not"=>omit_preconditions(value,!negated),
        ast::Expr::Binary{op,left,right,..} if matches!(op.as_str(),"and"|"or")=>{
            omit_preconditions(left,negated);omit_preconditions(right,negated);
        },
        ast::Expr::Binary{op,left,right,site}=>{
            let null=|e:&ast::Expr|matches!(e,ast::Expr::Literal{value:ast::Literal::Null,..});
            let stage=|field:&ast::Expr,value:&ast::Expr|plain_field(field)&&matches!(field,ast::Expr::Member{name,..} if matches!(name.as_str(),"stage"|"status"|"phase"|"state"))&&matches!(value,ast::Expr::Literal{value:ast::Literal::Str(_),..});
            let absence=(op=="="&&!negated||op=="!="&&negated)&&((null(left)&&plain_field(right))||(null(right)&&plain_field(left)));
            let discriminator=matches!(op.as_str(),"="|"!="|"<"|">"|"<="|">=")&&(stage(left,right)||stage(right,left));
            if absence||discriminator{*expression=ast::Expr::Literal{value:ast::Literal::Bool(true),site:site.clone()};}
        },
        // Unknown expression contexts retain every dependency. They do not
        // justify erasing a potentially positive evidence inspection.
        _=>{},
    }
}

pub fn is_derived(inv:&Invariant)->bool { inv.site.file.starts_with("$derived/") }
fn add(out:&mut Vec<Invariant>,table:&str,column:&str,target:&str,policy:RuleExpr,site:&Site){
    if target=="write" {
        add(out,table,column,"insert",RuleExpr::Or(vec![RuleExpr::Eq(RuleTerm::Field(column.into()),RuleTerm::Literal(snapback4_core::Value::Null)),policy.clone()]),site);
        add(out,table,column,"update",policy,site);return;
    }
    use sha2::{Digest,Sha256};
    let identity=format!("{:x}",Sha256::digest(serde_json::to_vec(&policy).unwrap()));
    let site=Site::new(&format!("$derived/{}/{table}/{column}/{target}/{identity}",site.file),site.line,site.col);
    if out.iter().any(|i|i.site==site&&i.policy.as_ref()==Some(&policy)){return;}
    out.push(Invariant{table:table.into(),column:Some(column.into()),target:target.into(),predicate:policy.quarry(),policy:Some(policy),site,operation:None,requirements:Vec::new(),syntax:None,unsupported:None});
}
pub fn invariants(sources:&[(String,String)],bound:&Bound)->Vec<Invariant>{
    let docs=sources.iter().map(|(f,s)|crate::parse_document(f,s).0).collect::<Vec<_>>();
    let catalog=Catalog::build(docs.clone(),&mut Vec::new());
    let mut out=Vec::new();
    let mut controlled=BTreeSet::new();
    for p in docs.iter().flat_map(|d|&d.programs).filter(|p|p.kind==ast::ProgramKind::Mutation){for (write,_) in authority_walk::writes(&catalog,p){
        if let ast::Expr::Write{op:ast::WriteOp::Insert|ast::WriteOp::Upsert,table,fields,..}=write{for f in fields{if write_advice::supplied(&f,p){controlled.insert((table.clone(),f.name));}}}
    }}
    for t in docs.iter().flat_map(|d|&d.tables){
        let Some(table)=bound.schema.table(&t.name)else{continue;};
        for (column,guard) in write_advice::table_guards(&table.rules.update){
            if controlled.contains(&(t.name.clone(),column.clone()))&&!table.immutable_fields().any(|c|c==&column)&&!table.column_writes.contains_key(&column)&&!write_advice::update_only(bound,&t.name,&column,&guard){add(&mut out,&t.name,&column,"write",guard,&t.site);}
        }
    }
    for p in docs.iter().flat_map(|d|&d.programs).filter(|p|p.kind==ast::ProgramKind::Mutation){
        let plain=authority_walk::writes(&catalog,p);
        for (write,context) in &plain {
            let ast::Expr::Write{op:ast::WriteOp::Update,table,fields,site,..}=write else{continue;};
            let Some(info)=catalog.tables.get(table)else{continue;};
            let definition=&bound.schema.tables[table];
            // Guarded edits: only the additional, column-specific condition.
            for field in fields {
                if definition.column_writes.contains_key(&field.name)||definition.immutable_fields().any(|c|c==&field.name){continue;}
                let specialized=authority_walk::column_writes(&catalog,p,&field.name);
                let mut policies=Vec::new();let mut conditional=false;
                for ((candidate,specific),(_,base)) in specialized.iter().zip(&plain){
                    if !matches!(candidate,ast::Expr::Write{op:ast::WriteOp::Update,table:t,fields,..} if t==table&&fields.iter().any(|f|f.name==field.name)){continue;}
                    conditional|=specific.body.len()>base.body.len();
                    let guards=operation::requirements(&catalog,info,specific);
                    policies.push(RuleExpr::And(guards.into_iter().filter(operation::safe).collect()));
                }
                let guard=RuleExpr::Or(policies);
                if conditional&&!write_advice::update_only(bound,table,&field.name,&guard){add(&mut out,table,&field.name,if controlled.contains(&(table.clone(),field.name.clone())){"write"}else{"update"},guard,site);}
            }
            // Approval candidates keep their inferred origin explicit. Only
            // values inspected by this approval (or media governed by its
            // stored approval timestamp) are proposed for freezing.
            let mut inspected=BTreeSet::new();
            for statement in &context.body {if let ast::Stmt::Require{condition,..}=statement {
                let mut e=condition.clone();
                // Absence and stage checks admit the transition; they do not
                // assert that the current value is evidence being approved.
                omit_preconditions(&mut e,false);
                super::super::intent_walk::expression(&mut e,&mut |e|if let ast::Expr::Member{base,name,..}=e {
                    let mut b=*base.clone();let mut same=false;super::super::intent_walk::expression(&mut b,&mut |x|if matches!(x,ast::Expr::Read(r) if &r.table==table){same=true;});
                    if same {inspected.insert(name.clone());}
                });
            }}
            for approval in fields.iter().filter(|f|["approv","authoriz"].iter().any(|s|f.name.to_lowercase().contains(s))){
                let Some(kind)=definition.columns.get(&approval.name)else{continue;};
                let condition=match (kind.required(),approval.value.as_ref()) {
                    (snapback4_core::schema::ColumnType::Bool,Some(ast::Expr::Literal{value:ast::Literal::Bool(true),..}))=>RuleExpr::Ne(RuleTerm::Field(approval.name.clone()),RuleTerm::Literal(snapback4_core::Value::Bool(true))),
                    (snapback4_core::schema::ColumnType::Time,Some(ast::Expr::Name{name,..})) if name=="now"=>RuleExpr::Eq(RuleTerm::Field(approval.name.clone()),RuleTerm::Literal(snapback4_core::Value::Null)),
                    _=>continue,
                };
                // A derived approve/edit contract concerns approval retained
                // across the edit. Deliberate invalidation is not a bypass.
                let paired=match &condition {
                    RuleExpr::Eq(RuleTerm::Field(c),v)=>RuleExpr::Or(vec![RuleExpr::Eq(RuleTerm::OldField(c.clone()),v.clone()),RuleExpr::Eq(RuleTerm::NextField(c.clone()),v.clone())]),
                    RuleExpr::Ne(RuleTerm::Field(c),v)=>RuleExpr::Or(vec![RuleExpr::Ne(RuleTerm::OldField(c.clone()),v.clone()),RuleExpr::Ne(RuleTerm::NextField(c.clone()),v.clone())]),_=>unreachable!("generated approval condition"),
                };
                for (column,_kind) in &definition.columns {
                    if !inspected.contains(column)||matches!(_kind.required(),snapback4_core::schema::ColumnType::Bool|snapback4_core::schema::ColumnType::Enum{..}|snapback4_core::schema::ColumnType::Principal|snapback4_core::schema::ColumnType::Ref{..}){continue;}
                    if fields.iter().any(|f|&f.name==column)||definition.immutable_fields().any(|c|c==column)||definition.frozen_columns.contains_key(column){continue;}
                    if !bound.programs.iter().any(|p|snapback4_core::ir::write_tables(&p.program).iter().any(|(t,e)|t==table&&matches!(*e,"update"|"upsert"))){continue;}
                    if !write_advice::update_only(bound,table,column,&condition){add(&mut out,table,column,"update",paired.clone(),site);}
                }
            }
        }
    }
    out
}
pub fn notes(sources:&[(String,String)],bound:&Bound)->Vec<Diagnostic>{
    invariants(sources,bound).into_iter().map(|i|Diagnostic::new(&i.site,"N_DERIVED_WRITE_AUTHORITY",format!("{}: derived candidate {} only {}; a replayed violation needs a specification decision",i.target_label(),i.target,i.predicate),"enforce column write/freeze authority, declare matching update-only creation intent, or record this individual rule's deliberate design with check --ack-security ID --reason '<spec rationale>'").guide("security")).collect()
}
pub fn augmented(sources:&[(String,String)],bound:&Bound)->Bound{
    let mut out=bound.clone();out.invariants.extend(invariants(sources,bound));
    // Declared write audiences are runtime contracts, not inferred spec intent.
    // Their replay failures cannot be acknowledged as deliberate asymmetry.
    let mut contracts=Vec::new();
    for (table,t) in bound.schema.visible_tables(){for (column,policy) in &t.column_writes{
        add(&mut contracts,table,column,"write",policy.clone(),&Site::new("column-write-contract",1,1));
    }}
    for contract in &mut contracts{contract.site.file=contract.site.file.replacen("$derived/","$enforced/",1);}
    out.invariants.extend(contracts);out
}
//! Stable semantic review bindings. Complex flow notes conservatively bind
//! their declaration dependency/writer closure, never the whole source set.
// @ref LLP 3000.036.004#173-presentation-and-review-identity — review survives independent edits.
use super::*;
use std::collections::BTreeSet;
use snapback4_lang::lexer::{lex,Kind};
#[derive(Clone)]
struct Declaration{file:String,line:u32,end:u32,name:String,kind:String,tokens:Vec<(String,String)>}
fn declarations(sources:&[(String,String)])->Vec<Declaration>{
    let mut out=Vec::new();
    for (file,text) in sources{
        let (tokens,_)=lex(file,text);
        let starts=tokens.iter().enumerate().filter(|(_,t)|t.site.col==1&&t.kind==Kind::Word).map(|(i,_)|i).collect::<Vec<_>>();
        for (n,start) in starts.iter().enumerate(){
            let end=starts.get(n+1).copied().unwrap_or(tokens.len());let ts=&tokens[*start..end];
            let Some(name)=ts.get(1).filter(|t|t.kind==Kind::Word)else{continue;};
            out.push(Declaration{file:file.clone(),line:ts[0].site.line,end:tokens.get(end).map_or(u32::MAX,|t|t.site.line),name:name.value.clone(),kind:ts[0].value.clone(),tokens:ts.iter().filter(|t|!matches!(t.kind,Kind::Newline|Kind::Eof)).map(|t|(format!("{:?}",t.kind),t.value.clone())).collect()});
        }
    }
    out
}
fn words(text:&str)->BTreeSet<&str>{text.split(|c:char|!c.is_alphanumeric()&&c!='_').filter(|s|!s.is_empty()).collect()}
fn stable_message(text:&str)->String{
    // Source sites in detector prose are evidence, not review identity.
    text.split_inclusive(char::is_whitespace).map(|chunk|{
        let word=chunk.trim_end_matches(char::is_whitespace);
        let suffix=&chunk[word.len()..];
        let parts=word.split(':').collect::<Vec<_>>();
        let normalized=if parts.len()>=3&&parts[parts.len()-2].parse::<u32>().is_ok()&&parts.last().unwrap().trim_end_matches([',',';',')']).parse::<u32>().is_ok(){parts[..parts.len()-2].join(":")}else{word.into()};
        format!("{normalized}{suffix}")
    }).collect()

}
fn strip_sites(value:&mut serde_json::Value){match value{
    serde_json::Value::Object(fields)=>{
        let metadata=fields.get("site").is_some_and(|site|site.is_string()||site.as_object().is_some_and(|site|site.len()==3&&site.contains_key("file")&&site.contains_key("line")&&site.contains_key("col")));
        if metadata{fields.remove("site");}
        for value in fields.values_mut(){strip_sites(value);}
    },
    serde_json::Value::Array(values)=>for value in values{strip_sites(value)},_=>{}
}}
pub(super) fn identities(notes:&[snapback4_lang::Diagnostic],sources:&[(String,String)])->Vec<(String,String,String)>{
    let decls=declarations(sources);
    let bound=snapback4_lang::compile(sources);
    let mut occurrences=BTreeMap::<String,usize>::new();
    notes.iter().map(|note|{
        let owner=decls.iter().find(|d|d.file==note.site.file&&d.line<=note.site.line&&note.site.line<d.end);
        let subject=owner.map(|d|format!("{}:{}:{}",d.file,d.kind,d.name)).unwrap_or_else(||note.site.file.clone());
        let base=hash(&serde_json::to_vec(&(ANALYZER,&note.code,&subject,stable_message(&note.message),&note.rewrite)).unwrap());
        let ordinal=occurrences.entry(base.clone()).or_default();let key=hash(&serde_json::to_vec(&(&base,*ordinal)).unwrap());*ordinal+=1;
        let mentioned=words(&note.message);
        let mut selected=decls.iter().enumerate().filter(|(_,d)|mentioned.contains(d.name.as_str())||owner.is_some_and(|o|std::ptr::eq(o,*d))).map(|(i,_)|i).collect::<BTreeSet<_>>();
        // Optional-column advice is exactly about that declaration's type and
        // rule; unrelated columns in the same table are not dependencies.
        let column=note.message.split_whitespace().next().and_then(|s|s.split_once('.')).filter(|(_,c)|!c.contains(':'));
        let precise=if note.code=="N_OPTIONAL_EVIDENCE"{column.and_then(|(table,column)|{
            sources.iter().find_map(|(file,text)|{
                let (doc,_)=snapback4_lang::parse_document(file,text);
                let c=doc.tables.iter().find(|t|t.name==table)?.columns.iter().find(|c|c.name==column)?;
                let (tokens,_)=lex(file,text);let tokens=tokens.iter().filter(|t|t.site.line==c.site.line&&!matches!(t.kind,Kind::Newline|Kind::Eof)).map(|t|(format!("{:?}",t.kind),t.value.clone())).collect::<Vec<_>>();
                Some(serde_json::to_vec(&(file,table,column,tokens)).unwrap())
            })
        })}else{None};
        let rule_binding=if matches!(note.code.as_str(),"N_INVARIANT_RESTATES_RULE"|"N_ROLE_WIDE_READ"|"N_BROAD_READ"){
            let label=note.message.split_whitespace().next().unwrap_or("").trim_end_matches(':');
            label.split_once('.').and_then(|(table,part)|{
                let t=bound.schema.tables.get(table)?;
                let column=t.columns.contains_key(part).then_some(part);
                let effect=if column.is_some(){note.message.split_whitespace().nth(1).unwrap_or("read").trim_end_matches(':')}else{part};
                let mut columns=column.into_iter().collect::<BTreeSet<_>>();
                if note.code=="N_ROLE_WIDE_READ"&&let Some(group)=note.message.split("protected columns [").nth(1).and_then(|s|s.split(']').next()){
                    columns.extend(group.split(',').map(str::trim).filter(|c|t.columns.contains_key(*c)));
                }
                let column_bindings=columns.iter().map(|c|(*c,serde_json::json!({"type":t.columns.get(*c),"read":t.column_reads.get(*c),"owner":t.written_by.get(*c)}))).collect::<BTreeMap<_,_>>();
                let mut value=serde_json::json!({"rowRead":t.rules.read,"columns":column_bindings,"effect":effect,
                    "invariants":bound.invariants.iter().filter(|i|i.table==table&&(i.column.is_none()&&column.is_none()||i.column.as_deref().is_some_and(|c|columns.contains(c)))&&(i.target==effect||i.target=="write")).collect::<Vec<_>>()});
                if effect!="read"{value["writeRules"]=serde_json::to_value(&t.rules).unwrap();value["owners"]=serde_json::to_value(&t.written_by).unwrap();}
                strip_sites(&mut value);
                let encoded=value.to_string();
                let mut dependencies=bound.schema.tables.keys().filter(|name|name.as_str()!=table&&encoded.contains(&format!("\"{name}\""))).cloned().collect::<BTreeSet<_>>();
                loop{let old=dependencies.clone();for name in &old{let serialized=serde_json::to_string(&bound.schema.tables[name]).unwrap();for candidate in bound.schema.tables.keys(){if candidate.as_str()!=table&&serialized.contains(&format!("\"{candidate}\"")){dependencies.insert(candidate.clone());}}}if dependencies==old{break;}}
                value["dependencies"]=serde_json::to_value(dependencies.iter().map(|name|(name,&bound.schema.tables[name])).collect::<BTreeMap<_,_>>()).unwrap();
                let mut writers=serde_json::to_value(bound.programs.iter().filter(|p|snapback4_core::ir::write_tables(&p.program).iter().any(|(table,_)|dependencies.contains(table))).map(|p|&p.program).collect::<Vec<_>>()).unwrap();strip_sites(&mut writers);value["dependencyWriters"]=writers;
                Some(serde_json::to_vec(&value).unwrap())
            })
        }else{None};
        let scope=if precise.is_some(){"exact column declaration"}else if rule_binding.is_some(){"effective rule/column and authority dependency closure"}else if selected.is_empty(){"whole-source fallback: unlocated/unknown subject"}else{"source declaration and dependency/writer closure (conservative)"};
        let fingerprint=if let Some(bytes)=precise.or(rule_binding){hash(&bytes)}else{
            // Forward names include helper bodies and referenced schemas.
            // Reverse writers include newly added mutators of a trusted grant.
            loop{
                let before=selected.clone();
                let names=selected.iter().map(|i|decls[*i].name.as_str()).collect::<BTreeSet<_>>();
                let dependencies=selected.iter().flat_map(|i|decls[*i].tokens.iter().filter(|(k,_)|k=="Word").map(|(_,v)|v.as_str())).collect::<BTreeSet<_>>();
                for (i,d) in decls.iter().enumerate(){
                    let module=std::path::Path::new(&d.file).file_stem().and_then(|s|s.to_str()).unwrap_or("");
                    let operation=if module=="schema"{d.name.clone()}else{format!("{module}.{}",d.name)};
                    let writer=matches!(d.kind.as_str(),"mutation"|"job")&&bound.programs.iter().find(|p|p.program.name==operation).is_some_and(|p|snapback4_core::ir::write_tables(&p.program).iter().any(|(table,_)|names.contains(table.as_str())));
                    let intent=d.kind=="invariant"&&names.contains(d.name.as_str());
                    if dependencies.contains(d.name.as_str())||writer||intent{selected.insert(i);}
                }
                if selected==before{break;}
            }
            if selected.is_empty(){
                // Unknown source ownership is not an empty authority binding.
                hash(&serde_json::to_vec(sources).unwrap())
            }else{
                let mut binding=selected.iter().map(|i|{let d=&decls[*i];(&d.file,&d.kind,&d.name,&d.tokens)}).collect::<Vec<_>>();binding.sort();
                hash(&serde_json::to_vec(&binding).unwrap())
            }
        };
        (key,fingerprint,scope.into())
    }).collect()
}

#[cfg(test)]mod tests{
use super::*;
use std::collections::BTreeSet;
fn note(line:u32,code:&str,message:&str)->snapback4_lang::Diagnostic{snapback4_lang::Diagnostic::new(&snapback4_lang::Site::new("schema.q",line,3),code,message,"review")}
fn ids(source:&str,notes:&[snapback4_lang::Diagnostic])->Vec<(String,String,String)>{identities(notes,&[("schema.q".into(),source.into())])}
const SOURCE:&str="table docs:\n  photo: image <=10000 ?\n  title: text <=100\n  read <- allow\n  insert <- allow\n  online only\nmutation add(photo:image <=10000 ?):\n  return insert docs {photo,title:'x'}\nquery unrelated():\n  return true\n";
#[test]fn independent_edits_and_comments_preserve_column_decisions(){
 let n=note(2,"N_OPTIONAL_EVIDENCE","docs.photo permits omitted or empty media evidence");let a=ids(SOURCE,&[n.clone()]);
 let changed=SOURCE.replace("title: text <=100","title: text <=200").replace("return true","return false");assert_eq!(a,ids(&changed,&[n.clone()]));
 let shifted=format!("-- a comment\n\n{SOURCE}");assert_eq!(a,ids(&shifted,&[note(4,&n.code,&n.message)]));
 let changed=SOURCE.replace("photo: image <=10000 ?","photo: image <=20000 ?");assert_ne!(a[0].1,ids(&changed,&[n])[0].1);
}
#[test]fn flow_decisions_bind_helpers_and_new_writers_but_not_independent_queries(){
 let n=note(8,"N_OPTIONAL_UPLOAD_CREATE","add: optional photo creates docs.photo");let a=ids(SOURCE,&[n.clone()]);
 assert_eq!(a,ids(&SOURCE.replace("return true","return false"),&[n.clone()]));
 let changed=format!("{SOURCE}mutation clear(doc:docs):\n  update docs[doc] {{photo:null}}\n");assert_ne!(a[0].1,ids(&changed,&[n.clone()])[0].1);
 let changed=SOURCE.replace("title:'x'","title:'y'");assert_ne!(a[0].1,ids(&changed,&[n])[0].1);
}
#[test]fn repeated_writes_keep_individual_ids_across_line_shifts(){
 let mut a=note(8,"N_OPTIONAL_UPLOAD_CREATE","add: optional photo creates docs.photo");let mut b=a.clone();b.site.line=9;
 let original=ids(SOURCE,&[a.clone(),b.clone()]);assert_ne!(original[0].0,original[1].0);
 a.site.line+=2;b.site.line+=2;assert_eq!(original,ids(&format!("-- comment\n\n{SOURCE}"),&[a,b]));
}
#[test]fn helpers_that_write_a_trusted_table_are_reverse_dependencies(){
 let s=SOURCE.to_owned()+"fn patch(doc:docs,title:text <=100):\n  update docs[doc] {title}\n  return null\nmutation edit(doc:docs):\n  return patch(doc,'a')\n";

exec
/bin/bash -lc "sed -n '2150,2189p' llp/3000.036.004-security-invariants-and-exposure.rfc.md; sed -n '2220,2285p' llp/3000.036.004-security-invariants-and-exposure.rfc.md; rg -n 'ack-security|security_reviews::review|decision_count|security_reviews::print|N_SECURITY_TEST|check:' snapback4/crates/snapback4/src --glob '*.rs'" in /home/ccheever/projects/sb4-b28-dx11
 succeeded in 0ms:
transition or derived creation; inconsistent server-now duration bounds on the
same bound column across operations; and global-role operations recording viewer
on an unrelated row. Restating questions and the draft ask whether each role may
write any row, independently of the read questionnaire. The relationship recipe
models the owner of this wall explicitly. These shapes cannot infer the spec.

### 16.2 Narrow gates and individual dispositions

N_OPTIONAL_UPLOAD_CREATE: an optional image/file mutation argument flows to an
insert/upsert of a media column, including a conditional child insertion. A
non-null guard around just the child does not establish required parent evidence.
An unconditional dominating require of non-null before any creating write can
exclude the note; aliases/helpers are resolved by the existing authority walker.
Optionality may be intentional: the gate requires review, never asserts an exploit.

N_RETAINED_ROLE_GRANT: a read branch trusts a retained grant; an authored grant
writer requires a literal role on the principal argument stored as that grant's
reader principal; that role is mutable through authored operations; and the read
branch does not imply the same current-reader role. Require a positive recipient
role atom in every admitting writer branch; do not mistake the assigner's role
for the recipient's. The gate excludes ordinary owner branches, literal service
seats and mere caller-role requirements. Revoking derived grants is a repair;
static inability to prove full revocation remains reviewable, not an automatic
safety theorem. General N_GRANT_OUTLIVES_ROLE stays advisory.

Unreviewed gates make check exit 1 with structured note IDs, without marking an
invariant refuted. The existing bulk --ack-notes cannot clear a security gate.
Use check --ack-security <id> --reason '<spec rationale>' for one current note.
Store versioned dispositions in snapback/security-reviews.json with ID, reason,
source digest and analyzer revision. IDs hash the complete diagnostic; the source
digest hashes all compiled source filenames and bytes in sorted order. Every
source edit, changed diagnostic or analyzer revision invalidates the disposition.
Blank reasons, unknown IDs, malformed files and incompatible flag combinations
refuse. Writes use temp+rename and verify compiled sources still match disk;
failed compilation/refuted intent must never record acknowledgments. Dispositions
are review records, never authority or proof, remain visible in JSON/audit and
can be checked into source control. Broad advice may also be individually reviewed.

### 16.3 Directed finite histories

Priority codes are fixed by security::note_priority: gates at 0; privilege and
self-claimed relationship at 1; grant-outlives, optional evidence, role-wide read,
broad read, caller time, sweep deadline and the seven new question codes at 2;
ordinary notes at 4; insert/result inventory at 5; restates at 6; intent coverage
at 7. The short list keeps every action at 0–2 plus the first five ordinary
code classes, preserving all sites/audiences within those classes. Repeated sites
of one ordinary code do not consume independent classes; insert/result codes
never enlarge the budget. This avoids hiding a second scan audience or a distinct
maintain diagnostic behind repeated bootstrap notes. Concrete
reachable refutations still print all failing sites, origins and repair commands
outside this list. A remainder count points to verbose/JSON. JSON retains every
note. Restates and coverage can appear only after the action classes fit.

Creation reason: an argument flows into an inserted/upserted column with `reason`
in its case-insensitive name and its text argument minimum is absent or zero.
Optional money: the optional argument flows into an optional Money column, or an
Int/IntRange/Decimal column whose lowercased name contains price, charge, fee or
amount. Constant approval: true literal assigned to a creating column containing
paid, approv or payment. All are name-based specification questions; no claim
covers other spellings. Required positive-minimum reasons and required money
arguments suppress their respective advice. One note per operation/column.

Transition bypass: same-table enum update from an enum argument can choose a
literal also written by another mutation, and its necessary effective admission
does not imply that other writer's bound requirements plus table gate. Resolve
row-local read operands to the target table's focal row for this advisory query.
A dominating contradictory literal argument guard excludes that target. Generic
setter/table/target/stricter-op duplicates collapse by exact diagnostic message.
A stricter-gate failure alone is advisory, never an inferred approval violation.
Stale creation: a resolved creating-write context reads a non-role dependency
that another mutation updates/upserts/deletes, and a different later mutation
updates the created table or writes a referencing child without reading that
dependency in its dominating context. Group by operation, created table and
dependency. Mere dependency reads do not prove eligibility; this heuristic's
false negatives and speculative questions are not gated.
Duration: compare the same resolved table/column against now +/- literal duration
with the same inequality direction in two operation contexts. Different offsets
produce one question listing all windows/operations. Other affine expressions,
custom wrappers and complex derived windows remain uncovered by this advice.
Role-wide write: a mutation updates a non-immutable Principal to viewer, has a
bound global role requirement, and lacks a dominating viewer-to-row-member
relationship comparison. It asks about this resource rather than declaring an
unauthorized write. The draft is bounded by discovered schema roles × tables;
it asks every pair about write authority, including pairs with authored intent.

Optional upload: the existing lexical authority walker resolves aliases and
helper arguments to the original mutation argument. Non-null suppression accepts
only a direct positive `argument != null` conjunct in an unconditional Require
before the first creating-write context. `$advice` branch guards cannot suppress;
late child guards, OR, unknown and unsupported expressions cannot suppress.
Known flows into asset fields are detected; arbitrary opaque/derived flows are
uncovered, never assumed safe. Both upsert arms retain the creation review question.
Retained role: use existing lints::mutable_role and privilege role-column/probe
resolution. Only direct positive Exists conjuncts with a literal recipient role
qualify, never an assigner/viewer-only test or negation. A branch suppresses only
when kernel::implies(branch, recipient-role-with-viewer) is Ok(true); false,
unknown and cap exhaustion retain the question. Dynamic role requirements stay
in general grant advice and are outside the narrow gate. Every noted branch
contains a positive grant probe binding that grant's principal column to viewer.
Owner equalities without such probes are excluded structurally. Role mutation
plus transactional grant revocation still needs review; it is not statically
proved safe or automatically refused as an exploit.

Disposition format is version 1: entries maps full diagnostic SHA256 IDs to
{sources, analyzer, reason}. JSON uses the existing Diagnostic serialization and
the sorted Vec<(filename, UTF-8 source bytes)> serialization used by Project.sources;
