//! CLI security reporting; compile/test evidence share one analyzer.
// @ref LLP 3000.036.004#5-exposure-report — full audit is opt-in, intent notes are on ordinary check.
use super::*;
use snapback4_lang::security::{self,Report};
mod http;
mod record;
mod presence;
#[cfg(test)] mod derived_tests;
pub(super) use presence::remember as remember_presence;
pub(super) use record::{remember,capture};
pub(super) fn unauthenticated(project:&Project,bound:&Bound,sources:&[(String,String)])->bool {
    match http::run(project,bound,sources){Ok(_)=>true,Err(why)=>{eprintln!("security HTTP failure: {why}");false}}
}
pub(super) fn report(project:&Project,bound:&Bound,sources:&[(String,String)],options:&Options)->Report{
    let augmented=if options.flags.contains_key("security-deploy"){security::derived::augmented(sources,bound)}else{bound.clone()};
    let bound=&augmented;
    let mut selection=options.clone();
    let selected_fixture=record::selected_fixture(project,options);
    if selected_fixture.is_dir(){selection.flags.insert("fixture".into(),selected_fixture.to_string_lossy().into_owned());}
    let inputs=match selection.flags.get("fixture"){
        Some(path)=>match super::test_fixture::snapshot(std::path::Path::new(path)){Ok(snapshot)=>Some(snapshot),Err(why)=>{let mut report=security::analyze_rules(bound);security::add_source_notes(&mut report,sources,bound);report.campaign_coverage.insert(format!("fixture reachability not covered: {why}"));return report;}},None=>None,
    };
    if let Some(snapshot)=&inputs{selection.flags.insert("fixture".into(),snapshot.0.to_string_lossy().into_owned());}
    let cached=record::matching(project,&selection,sources);
    let known_failure=cached.as_ref().is_some_and(|r|!r.violations.is_empty())&&!options.flags.contains_key("security-full");
    let mut campaign=if options.flags.contains_key("security-full"){security::challenges::Campaign::default()}else{if options.flags.contains_key("security-deploy"){let mut campaign=security::challenges::Campaign::bounded(10000);campaign.work_remaining=Some(8192);campaign}else{security::challenges::Campaign::bounded(1500)}};
    if known_failure {campaign.work_remaining=Some(0);campaign.unsupported.insert("matching full-test refutations retained; bounded resampling skipped; repair and rerun snapback4 test".into());}
    // An explicitly selected fixture remains the initial-state assumption.
    // Static generation runs only on the fixture-free path; both share this
    // one bounded campaign with all subsequent concrete exploration.
    let mut report=if selection.flags.contains_key("fixture")||known_failure||options.flags.contains_key("security-deploy"){
        security::analyze_rules(bound)
    }else{security::analyze_with_campaign(bound,&mut campaign)};
    security::add_source_notes(&mut report,sources,bound);
    if !bound.invariants.is_empty()&&!known_failure{
        if selection.flags.contains_key("fixture"){
            match super::test_fixture::engine_at(project,bound,&selection,1_800_000_000_000){
                Ok(fixture)=>if bound.schema.tables.keys().any(|t|fixture.store().rows(t).is_ok_and(|rs|!rs.is_empty())){security::challenges::fixture_observers(bound,&fixture,&mut campaign)}else{
                    // An empty starter directory selects generation, just as
                    // no fixture does. Give the direct contradiction its turn
                    // before empty-start mutation permutations spend the cap.
                    report=security::analyze_with_campaign(bound,&mut campaign);
                    security::add_source_notes(&mut report,sources,bound);
                    security::challenges::generated_campaign(bound,&mut campaign,false)
                },
                Err(why)=>{campaign.unsupported.insert(format!("fixture reachability not covered: {why}"));}
            }
        }else{security::challenges::generated_campaign(bound,&mut campaign,false);}
    }
    let sites=report.invariants.iter().filter(|v|v.status==security::Status::Unproved&&!campaign.violations.iter().any(|c|c.invariant_site==v.invariant.site.to_string()&&c.reachability.is_reachable())).map(|v|v.invariant.site.to_string()).collect();
    let campaign=security::challenges::run_selected_after(bound,Some(&sites),campaign);
    security::challenges::apply(&mut report,&campaign);
    report.notes.extend(presence::notes(project,bound,None,sources).unwrap_or_else(|why|vec![snapback4_lang::Diagnostic::new(&Default::default(),"N_PRESENCE_LOOSENING",why,"repair the unreadable generated presence baseline").guide("security")]));
    if let Some(record)=cached {security::challenges::apply(&mut report,&security::challenges::Campaign{violations:record.violations,..Default::default()});}
    report
}
pub fn audit(options:&Options)->i32{
    let project=project_or_exit();
    let sources=match project.sources(){Ok(s)=>s,Err(e)=>{eprintln!("audit: {e}");return 2;}};
    let bound=compiled_or_exit(snapback4_lang::compile(&sources),options.json);
    // Audit is a permission map plus bounded evidence; `test` owns full campaigns.
    let report=report(&project,&bound,&sources,options);
    let dispositions=match super::security_reviews::review(&project,&sources,&report.notes,None){Ok(d)=>d,Err(error)=>{eprintln!("audit: E_SECURITY_NOTES: {error}");return 2;}};
    if options.json {let mut value=serde_json::to_value(&report).unwrap();value["securityReviews"]=serde_json::to_value(&dispositions).unwrap();println!("{value}");}
    else {let mut human=report.clone();human.notes.retain(|n|crate::note_rank::tier(&n.code)!=crate::note_rank::Tier::Review);print!("{}",human.text_with_all(options.flags.contains_key("all")));super::security_reviews::print(&dispositions,&report.notes);}
    if report.refuted(){1}else{0}
}
pub(super) fn exposure(report:&Report){
    let broad=report.exposures.iter().filter(|e|e.effects.get("read").is_some_and(|r|r=="allow"||r.starts_with("public"))).count();
    eprintln!("security exposure: {} tables, {} conditional audiences, {broad} broadly readable tables; run snapback4 audit for exact role/column predicates",report.exposures.len(),report.roles.len());
}
// @ref LLP 3000.036.004#18-wave-26-security-decisions-dx9 — report enforcement facts without claiming complete security.
pub(super) fn enforced(report:&Report,bound:&Bound){
    let roles=report.notes.iter().filter(|n|n.code=="N_PRIVILEGE_ESCALATION").count();
    let columns=bound.schema.visible_tables().map(|(_,t)|t.column_reads.len()).sum::<usize>();
    let uploads=bound.schema.visible_tables().flat_map(|(_,t)|t.columns.values()).filter(|t|t.contains_asset()).count();
    println!("check: enforced — {uploads} typed upload columns with owner-bound placement; {columns} column audiences; {roles} possible self-assignable role paths detected (bounded analysis)");
}
pub(super) fn summary(report:&Report,verbose:bool){
    let proved=report.invariants.iter().filter(|v|v.status==security::Status::Proved).count();
    let failures=report.invariants.iter().filter(|v|v.status==security::Status::Refuted&&v.counterexample.as_ref().is_some_and(|c|c.reachability.is_reachable())).collect::<Vec<_>>();
    let refuted=report.invariants.iter().filter(|v|v.status==security::Status::Refuted).count();
    let rule_enforced=report.invariants.iter().filter(|v|v.status==security::Status::Unproved&&v.direct_rule==security::Status::Proved).count();
    eprintln!("security: {proved} proved, {refuted} refuted; {rule_enforced} enforced by rule (additional paths not proved); {} other unproved ({} failing)",report.invariants.len()-proved-refuted-rule_enforced,failures.len());
    for v in &failures {
        let c=v.counterexample.as_ref().unwrap();
        eprintln!("  FIX {}: {} via {} as {} ({}); tighten enforcement to the declared intent, then run snapback4 test",v.invariant.site,v.invariant.target_label(),c.operation,c.viewer,serde_json::to_value(&c.reachability).unwrap().as_str().unwrap());
    }
    let supplemental=report.invariants.iter().filter(|v|v.status==security::Status::Refuted&&v.counterexample.as_ref().is_some_and(|c|!c.reachability.is_reachable())).count();
    if supplemental>0 {eprintln!("  {supplemental} seed/synthetic observations (non-failing)");}
    if verbose {let mut human=report.clone();human.notes.retain(|n|crate::note_rank::tier(&n.code)!=crate::note_rank::Tier::Review);eprint!("{}",human.text_with_all(true));}
    else if !report.invariants.is_empty()||!report.campaign_coverage.is_empty(){eprintln!("  next: snapback4 test; {} coverage gaps (check --verbose/JSON keeps every reason)",report.campaign_coverage.len());}
    if !report.exposures.is_empty(){eprintln!("  review audiences and {} uncovered exposures: snapback4 audit",report.uncovered.len());}
}

/// A rule-changing preflight demands a current campaign, without pretending
/// finite tests or undeclared intent certify a deployment.
pub(super) fn release_loop(project:&Project,bound:&Bound,sources:&[(String,String)],prior:Option<&Bound>,options:&Options)->Result<Option<String>,String>{
    let changed=match prior {
        None=>true,
        Some(old)=>serde_json::to_vec(&old.schema).map_err(|e|e.to_string())?!=serde_json::to_vec(&bound.schema).map_err(|e|e.to_string())?
            ||serde_json::to_vec(&old.invariants).map_err(|e|e.to_string())?!=serde_json::to_vec(&bound.invariants).map_err(|e|e.to_string())?
            ||serde_json::to_vec(&old.programs.iter().map(|p|&p.program).collect::<Vec<_>>()).map_err(|e|e.to_string())?!=serde_json::to_vec(&bound.programs.iter().map(|p|&p.program).collect::<Vec<_>>()).map_err(|e|e.to_string())?,
    };
    if !changed{return Ok(None);}
    // @ref LLP 3000.036.004#153-rule-changing-deployment-review — review is prominent, strictness is explicit and failure-origin aware.
    let started=std::time::Instant::now();
    let mut preflight=options.clone();if !options.flags.contains_key("staging")||options.flags.contains_key("strict"){preflight.flags.insert("security-deploy".into(),String::new());}
    let mut report=report(project,bound,sources,&preflight);
    report.notes.extend(presence::notes(project,bound,prior,sources)?);
    if super::proof_source::sources(project,options,"schema")?!=sources{return Err("selected Quarry sources changed during deployment security preflight".into());}
    let dispositions=super::security_reviews::review_snapshot(project,sources,&report.notes)?;
    let derived=security::derived::notes(sources,bound);
    let acknowledged=derived.iter().filter(|n|report.notes.iter().zip(&dispositions).any(|(r,d)|r.site==n.site&&r.code==n.code&&r.message==n.message&&d.acknowledged)).map(|n|n.site.to_string()).collect::<std::collections::BTreeSet<_>>();
    let pending=super::security_reviews::pending(&dispositions);
    let fixes=report.invariants.iter().filter(|v|v.status==security::Status::Refuted&&v.counterexample.as_ref().is_some_and(|c|c.reachability.is_reachable())&&(!security::derived::is_derived(&v.invariant)||!acknowledged.contains(&v.invariant.site.to_string()))).count();
    eprintln!("security deploy campaign: {} ms; bounded replay, remaining paths retain coverage gaps",started.elapsed().as_millis());
    let scope=release_scope(bound,prior,sources);
    let mut notice=String::from("SECURITY REVIEW — rules changed; changed tables and operations only\n");
    for v in report.invariants.iter().filter(|v|v.status==security::Status::Refuted&&v.counterexample.as_ref().is_some_and(|c|c.reachability.is_reachable())){
        let c=v.counterexample.as_ref().unwrap();
        if security::derived::is_derived(&v.invariant)&&acknowledged.contains(&v.invariant.site.to_string()){
            notice.push_str(&format!("  REVIEWED derived {}: individual specification decision retained\n",v.invariant.target_label()));continue;
        }
        notice.push_str(&format!("  FIX {} {}: refuted via {} as {} ({:?}); repair enforcement to match intent, then run snapback4 test\n",v.invariant.site,v.invariant.target_label(),c.operation,c.viewer,c.reachability));
        if !security::derived::is_derived(&v.invariant) {
            if v.invariant.target=="write"&&v.invariant.column.is_some()&&c.effect=="insert" {
                notice.push_str(&format!("  INTENT {}: if broader creation is deliberate, replace this assertion with `invariant {}.{} update only {}`; then run snapback4 test. Authored contradictions cannot be cleared with --ack-security\n",v.invariant.site,v.invariant.table,v.invariant.column.as_ref().unwrap(),v.invariant.predicate));
            }else {
                notice.push_str(&format!("  INTENT {}: if this observed audience is deliberate, revise the authored assertion here to match that specification, then run snapback4 test; --ack-security applies only to derived questions, never an authored contradiction\n",v.invariant.site));
            }
        }
        let aggregates=bound.schema.maintains.iter().filter(|m|m.source==v.invariant.table).map(|m|m.target.as_str()).collect::<Vec<_>>();
        if c.observations.is_some()&&!aggregates.is_empty()&&v.invariant.target=="read"&&v.invariant.column.is_none() {
            notice.push_str(&format!("  DERIVED {}: a separately readable aggregate changes in the paired observation; tightening the source read rule alone cannot clear this disclosure\n",aggregates.join(", ")));
            let field=v.invariant.predicate.split('.').skip(1).filter_map(|part|part.split(|c:char|!c.is_alphanumeric()&&c!='_').next()).find(|field|bound.schema.table(&v.invariant.table).and_then(|t|t.columns.get(*field)).is_some_and(|kind|matches!(kind.required(),snapback4_core::schema::ColumnType::Principal)));
            if let Some(field)=field {notice.push_str(&format!("  INTENT {}: if aggregate counts are intentionally public, replace the whole-row assertion with `invariant {}.{} read only {}` for this protected field and independently declare any other private fields; otherwise restrict the aggregate audience; rerun snapback4 test\n",v.invariant.site,v.invariant.table,field,v.invariant.predicate));}
        }else if v.invariant.target=="read"&&v.invariant.column.is_none() {
            for (file,text) in sources {
                let doc=snapback4_lang::parser::parse_document(file,text).0;
                if let Some(table)=doc.tables.iter().find(|t|t.name==v.invariant.table) {
                    if let Some(rule)=table.rules.iter().find(|r|r.effect==snapback4_lang::ast::Effect::Read) {
                        let expanded=bound.refusal_sites.get(&(v.invariant.table.clone(),"$read-rule".into()));
                        if expanded.is_some() {
                            notice.push_str(&format!("  SOURCE {}: preserve `{}` and its parent lifetime/authority; if this audience is unintended, repair the parent read rule before rerunning snapback4 test\n",v.invariant.table,snapback4_lang::bind::spell_internals(&snapback4_lang::print::expression(&rule.value))));
                        }else {
                            notice.push_str(&format!("  SOURCE {}: retain the current audience and enforce this declared intent with `read <- ({}) and ({})`; rerun snapback4 test\n",v.invariant.table,snapback4_lang::print::expression(&rule.value),v.invariant.predicate));
                        }
                        let predicate=expanded.cloned().unwrap_or_else(||snapback4_lang::print::expression(&rule.value));
                        notice.push_str(&format!("  INTENT {}: if every reader admitted by the current read rule is deliberately allowed, replace this assertion with `invariant {}.read only {predicate}`; then run snapback4 test and deploy again. This is an authored specification change, not --ack-security\n",v.invariant.site,v.invariant.table));
                    }
                }
            }
        }

    }
    let mut notes=report.notes.iter().filter(|n|n.code!="N_SECURITY_INTENT"&&scope.iter().any(|(file,start,end)|n.site.file==*file&&n.site.line>=*start&&n.site.line<*end)).collect::<Vec<_>>();
    notes.sort_by_key(|n|crate::note_rank::summary_order(&n.code));
    let count=notes.len();
    // Never suppress a gate or write-authority question behind the display cap.
    for (i,n) in notes.into_iter().enumerate(){if crate::note_rank::tier(&n.code)!=crate::note_rank::Tier::Review&&(i<5||security::note_priority(&n.code)<=3){notice.push_str(&format!("  REVIEW {} {}: {}\n    {}\n",n.site,n.code,n.message,n.rewrite));}}
    if count>5 {notice.push_str(&format!("  {count} changed-scope notes in deploy --check --json\n"));}
    let selected=report.notes.iter().zip(&dispositions).filter(|(n,d)|d.gated || n.code!="N_SECURITY_INTENT" && scope.iter().any(|(file,start,end)| n.site.file==*file&&n.site.line>=*start&&n.site.line<*end)).collect::<Vec<_>>();
    let complete_family=dispositions.iter().filter(|d|d.family.is_some()).count()==selected.iter().filter(|(_,d)|d.family.is_some()).count();
    let scoped_notes=selected.iter().map(|(n,_)|(*n).clone()).collect::<Vec<_>>();
    let scoped_dispositions=selected.iter().map(|(_,d)| {let mut d=(*d).clone(); if !complete_family {d.family=None;} d}).collect::<Vec<_>>();
    // A scoped deploy cannot silently acknowledge unseen family members. It
    // prints their individual commands; ordinary check offers the whole family.
    notice.push_str(&super::security_reviews::render(&scoped_dispositions,&scoped_notes));
    let test=release_notice(project,bound,sources,options);
    notice.push_str(&format!("  security exposure: {} tables; {} independent invariants; {} coverage gaps\n  {}",report.exposures.len(),bound.invariants.len(),report.campaign_coverage.len(),test));
    let verdict=format!("security FIX {fixes}; {} pending review; {test}",dispositions.iter().filter(|d|d.gated&&!d.acknowledged).count());
    Ok(Some(serde_json::json!({"notice":notice,"verdict":verdict,"fixes":fixes,"pending":pending,"changedNotes":count,"report":report,"securityReviews":dispositions}).to_string()))
}
fn age(timestamp:Option<i64>,now:i64)->String{
    timestamp.map(|t|format!("at {t} ms since Unix epoch, age {}s",now.saturating_sub(t).max(0)/1000)).unwrap_or_else(||"time/age unknown (legacy record)".into())
}
fn release_notice(project:&Project,bound:&Bound,sources:&[(String,String)],options:&Options)->String{
    let selection=options.flags.get("schema").map(|s|format!(" --source {s:?}")).unwrap_or_default();
    let fixture=options.flags.get("fixture").map(|s|format!(" --fixture {s:?}")).unwrap_or_default();
    let now=snapback4_core::now_ms();
    let matching=record::matching(project,options,sources);
    let matches=matching.is_some();
    match matching.or_else(||record::latest(project)){
        Some(record)=>{
            let result=if !record.complete{"incomplete"}else if record.run_violations.unwrap_or(record.violations.len())>0{"FAILED"}else{"completed, no observed violation"};
            let current=if matches{"matches selected source/fixtures/CLI"}else{"STALE: source, fixtures or CLI differ; does not qualify selected release"};
            let next=if matches&&record.complete{"matching test campaign recorded (finite, not proof)".into()}else{format!("run `snapback4 test{selection}{fixture}` before deployment")};
            format!("last test: {result}, {}; {current}; {} retained reachable refutations, {} coverage gaps; last completed test {}; {next}",age(record.recorded_at,now),record.violations.len(),record.coverage_gaps,age(record.last_completed_at,now))
        },
        None=>format!("last test: NEVER RECORDED; run `snapback4 test{selection}{fixture}` before deployment; no matching completed campaign for this source, fixtures and CLI; {} independent invariants",bound.invariants.len()),
    }
}

// SEC7 owns ordinary check ranking/gates; this scope and output belong to ROLL.
fn release_scope(bound:&Bound,prior:Option<&Bound>,sources:&[(String,String)])->Vec<(String,u32,u32)> {
    let mut scope=Vec::new();
    fn normalized(p:&impl serde::Serialize)->serde_json::Value {
        fn clear(v:&mut serde_json::Value){match v{serde_json::Value::Object(o)=>{o.remove("site");for v in o.values_mut(){clear(v);}},serde_json::Value::Array(a)=>for v in a{clear(v)},_=>()}}
        let mut v=serde_json::to_value(p).unwrap();clear(&mut v);v
    }
    for(file,text)in sources {
        let doc=snapback4_lang::parser::parse_document(file,text).0;
        let mut starts=text.lines().enumerate().filter(|(_,s)|!s.is_empty()&&!s.starts_with(char::is_whitespace)&&!s.starts_with("--")).map(|(i,_)|i as u32+1).collect::<Vec<_>>();starts.sort();
        let mut add=|line:u32|scope.push((file.clone(),line,starts.iter().copied().find(|n|*n>line).unwrap_or(u32::MAX)));
        for t in &doc.tables {if prior.is_none_or(|old|old.schema.tables.get(&t.name).map(normalized)!=bound.schema.tables.get(&t.name).map(normalized)){add(t.site.line);}}
        for p in &doc.programs {
            let next=bound.programs.iter().find(|b|b.program.name==p.name);
            let old=prior.and_then(|b|b.programs.iter().find(|b|b.program.name==p.name));
            if next.map(|b|normalized(&b.program))!=old.map(|b|normalized(&b.program)){add(p.site.line);}
        }
    }scope
}
pub(super) fn print_release(summary:Option<&String>){if let Some(s)=summary.and_then(|s|serde_json::from_str::<serde_json::Value>(s).ok()){eprintln!("{}",s["notice"].as_str().unwrap_or(""));}}
pub(super) fn release_verdict(summary:Option<&String>)->String {summary.and_then(|s|serde_json::from_str::<serde_json::Value>(s).ok()).and_then(|s|s["verdict"].as_str().map(str::to_owned)).unwrap_or_else(||"security unchanged".into())}

pub(super) fn enforce_release(summary:Option<&String>,options:&Options)->Result<(),String>{
    let Some(value)=summary.and_then(|s|serde_json::from_str::<serde_json::Value>(s).ok())else{return Ok(())};
    if !options.flags.contains_key("staging")||options.flags.contains_key("strict"){
        if value["fixes"].as_u64().unwrap_or(0)>0{return Err("E_SECURITY_REVIEW: production deploy refuses reachable FIX; repair the named rule and rerun snapback4 test; for a derived candidate only, run check --ack-security ID --reason '<specification rationale>' using the reported rule ID".into())}
        if value["pending"].as_bool()==Some(true){return Err("E_SECURITY_REVIEW: production deploy requires individual security review; run each reported check --ack-security ID --reason '<specification decision>', or enforce the named intent in source".into())}
    }Ok(())
}

    10	
    11	## 19. Wave-28 output and witness decisions (DX11)
    12	
    13	Commissioned by Charlie Cheever, 2026-10-25. This amendment supersedes
    14	§18's default questionnaire and exact-set family command contract. It does
    15	not change SEC10's detector or gate semantics.
    16	
    17	A passing ordinary `check` prints one verdict line and the existing bounded
    18	language-enforcement summary. Only gates and reachable authored-invariant
    19	refutations block. Advisory decisions and compiler notes require `check
    20	--review` (also `--verbose` or JSON); the verdict counts them and names that
    21	command. A pending gate always prints its individual command. JSON keeps all
    22	sites, IDs, bindings, acknowledgments and evidence, regardless of display mode.
    23	
    24	Decision identity excludes incidental locations and wall-clock campaign
    25	results. A single canonical note set feeds JSON, display and acknowledgment.
    26	Repeated `--ack-security ID` and comma-separated IDs with one `--reason` are
    27	validated completely and committed atomically under the existing lock/source
    28	race checks. An invalid member writes nothing. An audience family command
    29	expands only to current advisory members; prior member records survive member
    30	additions. Each new or changed member remains pending. Previously displayed
    31	family aliases may acknowledge only their still-current original members;
    32	never silently acknowledge a newly added member. Unknown IDs explain that
    33	they are stale, from a different source selection, or not decision IDs and
    34	point to `check --review --json`. Relevant semantic dependency changes still
    35	invalidate individual acknowledgments; unrelated declarations and comments do
    36	not. Version-1 records remain readable; witness-bearing decisions require a
    37	new analyzer revision so old rationales cannot silently close new evidence.
    38	
    39	A concrete campaign result associated with a question is shown as a witness:
    40	operation, caller, arguments, admitted effect, old/new image and candidate
    41	invariant. It remains evidence about an inferred specification question,
    42	never an authored contradiction or proof. Unsupported or exhausted campaigns
    43	say no witness was obtained, without inventing behavior. Acknowledgment
    44	records retain the displayed witness and candidate, alongside the rationale
    45	and semantic binding. Upsert wording explicitly says that its existing-row
    46	arm **edits**. Derived evidence cannot be used to override a reachable authored
    47	invariant refutation. No credentials enter decision records or output.
    48	
    49	The adjoining developer-loop changes preserve the contracts of LLP
    50	3000.000.000.001 and LLP 3000.015: refusal attribution and read-filter evidence
    51	are developer-owned diagnostics, never new disclosure in public refusal
    52	responses. Hooks expose `usePage.loading` and `usePage.why` as projections of
    53	`page.read`, retaining its completeness/freshness evidence. A first-run check
    54	hint makes the pinned React starter discoverable for projects that skipped
    55	init, without modifying their files. Staging reports name persisted clock
    56	offset; upload overload guidance names serialization/retry; proof docs say
    57	text must be on screen, and verification recipes use `serve-check`. Expo's
    58	starter offers a web file picker and native image picker.
    59	
    60	Qualification: targeted CLI/client regressions, witnesses for wave-28 011 F1,
    61	011 F2 and 019 F1, copies of all fifty schemas with baseline and SEC10 codes,
    62	packed consumer snippets/starters, affected selector and reference check.
    63	Darwin is unavailable in this Linux lane and is explicitly skipped. Finite
    64	campaign replay is not a security proof or a promise of builder preference.
    65	Two independent reviews (Muse and fresh Codex) are retained under llp/reviews.
    66	
    67	## 18. Wave-26 security decisions (DX9)
    68	
    69	Implementer: Codex for Charlie Cheever, 2026-10-24. Revision 3: Muse and fresh Codex READY; implementation is in the DX9 commits. Qualification and limitations: WORKSTREAM-REPORT.md at the worktree root.
    70	This supersedes §17.3's prohibition on family disposition only for the

//! Concrete, isolated challenges; finite campaigns never establish proof.
// @ref LLP 3000.036.004#4-counterexamples-and-generated-tests — only replayed admission is a refutation.
use super::*;
use snapback4_core::{Engine,Store,Context,Value,RuntimeError};
use snapback4_core::schema::{ColumnType as C,Constraint};
use snapback4_core::engine::rules::{self,Probe};
use snapback4_core::ir::{self,Expr,Stmt,Program,ProgramKind,Read,KeyProvenance};
pub const NOW:i64=1_800_000_000_000;
const VIEWER:&str="dev:security-a";
const OTHER:&str="dev:security-b";
// Same verified 1x1 PNG header used by the existing packaged asset witnesses.
const PNG:[u8;24]=[137,80,78,71,13,10,26,10,0,0,0,13,73,72,68,82,0,0,0,1,0,0,0,1];
mod paired;
mod timed;
mod histories;
mod executed;
mod assets;
mod column_reads;
mod stability;
mod generated;
pub use generated::fixture as generated_fixture;
#[derive(Clone,Debug,Serialize,serde::Deserialize,PartialEq,Eq)]
#[serde(rename_all="kebab-case")]
pub enum Reachability { AuthoredFixtureTrace, GeneratedFixtureTrace, TrustedSeedOnly, SyntheticOperation }
impl Reachability {
    pub fn is_reachable(&self)->bool {matches!(self,Self::AuthoredFixtureTrace|Self::GeneratedFixtureTrace)}
}
#[derive(Clone,Debug,Serialize,serde::Deserialize)]
pub struct Counterexample {pub reachability:Reachability,pub invariant_site:String,pub args:BTreeMap<String,Value>,pub viewer:String,pub operation:String,pub effect:String,pub row:Value,pub old:Option<Value>,pub supporting_rows:BTreeMap<String,Vec<Value>>,pub rule_path:String,pub scope:String, pub observations:Option<paired::Observations>,pub history:Vec<histories::Step>}
#[derive(Clone,Debug,Default,Serialize)]
pub struct Campaign {#[doc(hidden)] #[serde(skip)] pub time_samples:Option<Vec<Value>>,pub evaluation_unknowns:BTreeSet<String>,pub directed_attempts:usize,pub directed_peak_worlds:usize,#[serde(skip)] pub history_limit:usize,#[serde(skip)] pub generated_fixture:bool,#[serde(skip)] pub work_remaining:Option<usize>,#[serde(skip)] pub deadline:Option<std::time::Instant>,pub paired_observer_calls:usize,pub history_observer_calls:usize,pub column_observer_calls:usize,pub operations:BTreeMap<String,usize>,pub sequences:usize,pub attempted:usize,pub admitted:usize,pub refused:usize,pub violations:Vec<Counterexample>,pub boundary_failures:Vec<String>,pub unsupported:BTreeSet<String>}
impl Campaign {
    pub fn bounded(milliseconds:u64)->Self {Self{work_remaining:Some(512),deadline:Some(std::time::Instant::now()+std::time::Duration::from_millis(milliseconds)),..Self::default()}}
    pub(super) fn reserve(&mut self,cost:usize)->bool {
        if self.exhausted(){return false;}
        if let Some(remaining)=&mut self.work_remaining {
            if *remaining<cost{*remaining=0;self.unsupported.insert("check concrete exploration work budget exhausted; remaining paths unproved; run snapback4 test for the full campaign".into());return false;}
            *remaining-=cost;
        }true
    }
    pub(super) fn exhausted(&mut self)->bool {
        if self.work_remaining==Some(0){self.unsupported.insert("check concrete exploration work budget exhausted; remaining paths unproved; run snapback4 test for the full campaign".into());return true;}
        if self.deadline.is_some_and(|deadline|std::time::Instant::now()>=deadline){self.unsupported.insert("check concrete exploration time budget exhausted; remaining paths unproved; run snapback4 test for the full campaign".into());true}else{false}
    }
}
fn id(table:&str)->Value {Value::Id(format!("security-{table}"))}
fn sample(kind:&C,variant:usize)->Option<Value>{Some(match kind{
    C::Optional(inner)=>if variant&4!=0{sample(inner,variant).unwrap_or(Value::Null)}else{Value::Null},C::Id|C::Principal=>Value::Id(if variant&1==0{VIEWER}else{OTHER}.into()),
    C::Ref{table}=>id(table),C::Text{max}=>Value::String("x".repeat((*max).min(20) as usize)),
    C::Enum{variants}=>Value::String(variants.get(variant%variants.len())?.clone()),
    C::Int|C::Decimal{..}|C::Money{..}=>Value::Int(1),C::IntRange{min,max}=>Value::Int(1i64.clamp(*min,*max)),
    C::Bool=>Value::Bool((variant>>2)&1==0),C::Time=>Value::Int(NOW-1000),C::Json{..}=>Value::Null,C::Bytes{..}=>Value::Bytes(Vec::new()),
    C::List{..}=>Value::Array(Vec::new()),C::ListRange{of,min,..}=>Value::Array((0..*min).map(|_|sample(of,variant)).collect::<Option<_>>()?),
    C::Image{..}=>Value::String("$security:image".into()),C::File{..}=>Value::String("$security:file".into()),C::Video{..}=>return None,
})}
fn needed(schema:&Schema,name:&str,out:&mut BTreeSet<String>){
    if !out.insert(name.into()){return;}let Some(t)=schema.table(name)else{return};
    for m in schema.maintains.iter().filter(|m|m.target==name){needed(schema,&m.source,out);}
    for k in t.columns.values(){if let C::Ref{table}=k.required(){needed(schema,table,out);}}
    for r in super::columns::rules(t){for dep in kernel::dependencies(r){needed(schema,&dep,out);}}
}
fn snapshot(bound:&Bound,inv:&Invariant,variant:usize)->Result<BTreeMap<String,Vec<Value>>,String>{
    snapshot_mode(bound,inv,variant,false)
}
fn snapshot_mode(bound:&Bound,inv:&Invariant,variant:usize,timed:bool)->Result<BTreeMap<String,Vec<Value>>,String>{
    let mut names=BTreeSet::new();needed(&bound.schema,&inv.table,&mut names);
    if let Some(policy)=&inv.policy{for dep in kernel::dependencies(policy){needed(&bound.schema,&dep,&mut names);}}
    if let Some(mut syntax)=inv.syntax.clone(){super::super::intent_walk::expression(&mut syntax,&mut |e| {
        if let ast::Expr::Read(r)|ast::Expr::Exists{read:r,..}=e {needed(&bound.schema,&r.table,&mut names);}
    });}
    if let Some(op)=&inv.operation && let Some(p)=bound.programs.iter().find(|p|&p.program.name==op){for kind in p.program.args.values(){if let ir::ArgType::Ref{table}=kind{needed(&bound.schema,table,&mut names);}}}
    let mut out=BTreeMap::new();
    let mut enum_case=variant;
    for name in names {
        let t=&bound.schema.tables[&name];
        if bound.schema.maintain_for(&name).is_some(){continue;}
        let mut fields=BTreeMap::new();fields.insert("id".into(),id(&name));
        let mut principal_index=0usize;
        for (n,k) in &t.columns{
            if n=="id"||t.engine_field(n)||t.maintained.contains_key(n){continue;}
            let sample_variant=if let C::Enum{variants}=k.required(){let n=enum_case % variants.len();enum_case/=variants.len();n}else if matches!(k.required(),C::Principal){let n=((variant>>((if name==inv.table{0}else{3})+principal_index.min(5)))&1)|(variant&4);principal_index+=1;n}else{variant};
            let value=sample(k,sample_variant)
                .ok_or_else(||format!("{name}.{n}: asset ledger/required sample is not available"))?;
            fields.insert(n.clone(),value);
        }
        if timed || t.read_lifetime_sensitive() {for (n,k) in &t.columns {if matches!(k.required(),C::Time)&&(!k.is_optional()||variant&4!=0) {
            fields.insert(n.clone(),Value::Int(NOW+if t.expire.as_ref()==Some(n){2000}else if timed{1000}else{-1000}));
        }}}
        for constraint in &t.constraints{if let Constraint::Text{field,min,..}=constraint
            && matches!(fields.get(field),Some(Value::String(_))) {fields.insert(field.clone(),Value::String("x".repeat((*min).max(1) as usize)));}}
        // Named primary keys used by role probes must select the challenged
        // viewer; changing the enum represents a current-role change while
        // derived assignment/membership rows remain present.
        if name!=inv.table {for (i,(n,k)) in t.columns.iter().filter(|(_,k)|matches!(k,C::Principal)).enumerate(){fields.insert(n.clone(),Value::Id(if variant & (1 << (i+3)) == 0 {VIEWER}else{OTHER}.into())); let _=k;}}
        out.insert(name,vec![Value::Row(fields.into())]);
    }
    Ok(out)
}
struct Snapshot<'a>{schema:&'a Schema,rows:&'a BTreeMap<String,Vec<Value>>,now:i64}
impl Probe for Snapshot<'_>{
    fn exists(&mut self,table:&str,index:&str,key:&[Value],equalities:&[(String,Value)])->Result<bool,RuntimeError>{
        let t=&self.schema.tables[table];let Some(idx)=t.index(index)else{return Ok(false)};
        if idx.components.len()!=key.len(){return Ok(false);}
        if idx.unique&&idx.components.iter().any(|c|c.column().is_some_and(|n|t.columns.get(n).is_some_and(C::is_optional)))&&key.contains(&Value::Null){return Ok(false);}
        Ok(self.rows.get(table).into_iter().flatten().filter(|r|!t.expired(r,self.now)&&t.not_before.as_ref().is_none_or(|c|matches!(r.field(c),Ok(Value::Null))||matches!(r.field(c),Ok(Value::Int(n)) if *n<=self.now))).any(|r|idx.components.iter().zip(key).all(|(c,v)|c.column().is_some_and(|n|r.field(n).ok()==Some(v)))&&equalities.iter().all(|(n,v)|r.field(n).ok()==Some(v))))
    }
    fn created(&mut self,_:&str,_:&str,_:&[Value])->bool{false}

}
fn live(t:&snapback4_core::schema::Table,row:&Value)->bool{
    !t.expired(row,NOW)&&t.not_before.as_ref().is_none_or(|c|match row.field(c){Ok(Value::Int(n))=>*n<=NOW,Ok(Value::Null)=>true,_=>false})
}
fn policy(bound:&Bound,inv:&Invariant,rows:&BTreeMap<String,Vec<Value>>,viewer:&str,old:Option<&Value>,row:&Value)->Result<bool,String>{policy_at(bound,inv,rows,viewer,old,row,NOW)}
fn policy_in(bound:&Bound,inv:&Invariant,rows:&BTreeMap<String,Vec<Value>>,viewer:&str,old:Option<&Value>,row:&Value,engine:&mut Engine)->Result<bool,String>{policy_in_at(bound,inv,rows,viewer,old,row,engine,NOW)}
fn policy_at(bound:&Bound,inv:&Invariant,rows:&BTreeMap<String,Vec<Value>>,viewer:&str,old:Option<&Value>,row:&Value,now:i64)->Result<bool,String>{
    if super::write_intent::empty_creation(bound,inv,old,row){return Ok(true);}

    if inv.policy.as_ref().is_some_and(|p|kernel::dependencies(p).iter().any(|d|bound.schema.tables.get(d).is_some_and(|t|t.through.is_some()))) {
        return inv.syntax.as_ref().and_then(|e|super::concrete::evaluate(e,row,rows,&bound.schema,viewer,&inv.table,now)).ok_or_else(||"logical read-through invariant judgment is unknown".into());
    }
    let Some(p)=inv.policy.as_ref() else{return inv.syntax.as_ref().and_then(|e|super::concrete::evaluate(e,row,rows,&bound.schema,viewer,&inv.table,now)).ok_or_else(||"concrete policy expression is unknown/unsupported (including clock and typed literal coercions)".into());};

    let mut image=row.clone();if super::temporal::has_clock(p)&&let Value::Row(fields)=&mut image{fields.insert("$security:server-clock".into(),Value::Int(now));}
    if p.has_reference(){return Err("reference intent requires authorized engine policy replay".into());}
    rules::evaluate(p,viewer,old,&image,false,&mut Snapshot{schema:&bound.schema,rows,now}).map_err(|e|e.to_string())
}
fn policy_in_at(bound:&Bound,inv:&Invariant,rows:&BTreeMap<String,Vec<Value>>,viewer:&str,old:Option<&Value>,row:&Value,engine:&mut Engine,now:i64)->Result<bool,String>{
    if super::write_intent::empty_creation(bound,inv,old,row){return Ok(true);}
    let Some(p)=&inv.policy else{return policy_at(bound,inv,rows,viewer,old,row,now);};
    if !p.has_reference(){return policy_at(bound,inv,rows,viewer,old,row,now);}
    let mut image=row.clone();if super::temporal::has_clock(p)&&let Value::Row(fields)=&mut image{fields.insert("$security:server-clock".into(),Value::Int(now));}
    match engine.fixture_policy(p,viewer,old,&image,now).map_err(|e|e.to_string())?{
        rules::Verdict::Readable=>Ok(true),rules::Verdict::Denied=>Ok(false),rules::Verdict::Unknown=>Err("policy reference/coverage judgment is unknown; never a refutation".into()),
    }
}
fn judged(inv:&Invariant,result:Result<bool,String>,out:&mut Campaign)->Result<bool,String>{
    if let Err(why)=&result{out.evaluation_unknowns.insert(format!("{} at {}: {why}",inv.target_label(),inv.site));}
    result
}
fn program(name:&str,kind:ProgramKind,body:Vec<Stmt>)->Program{Program{name:name.into(),kind,args:BTreeMap::new(),arg_aliases:BTreeMap::new(),body,read_time_granularity_ms:None,returned_shapes:BTreeMap::new(),native:false}}
# Security invariants

State privacy intent separately from enforcement. Check reports proved, refuted
with a replayed counterexample, or unproved with a reason; passing finite tests
are not proofs.

```quarry
table privateNotes:
  patient: principal
  body: text 1..2000
  sealed: bool default false
  read <- .patient = viewer and .sealed = false
  insert <- .patient = viewer and .sealed = false
  online only
-- Spec: a patient may see only their own note; sealing may narrow availability.
-- Removing an accidental broad branch from the rule must not change this intent.
invariant privateNotes.read only .patient = viewer
invariant privateNotes.body read only .patient = viewer
invariant privateNotes.insert only .patient = viewer
query privateNote(note: privateNotes):
  return privateNotes[note]
mutation writePrivateNote(body: text 1..2000):
  return insert privateNotes { patient: viewer, body }
```

Run `snapback4 check` → `snapback4 test` → `snapback4 audit`. Check reports
invariant counts, five ordinary note classes and all likely-hole questions; `--verbose` retains every reason.
Answer the rule questions from the product specification before editing intent.
The restating advisory names each admitted role and its row conditions: for
example, a coach branch with no assignment check asks whether coaches should
see other coaches' athletes. For every role, answer which rows, which columns,
and which status/consent conditions the spec permits. Include derived values
and downloads: hiding a diagnosis but exposing its category can reveal it.
Write that answer as independent read intent; repair a rule that admits more.
A matching or weaker assertion may still be the right simple owner policy:
keep confirmed intent. The advisory never refuses it or changes its verdict.
Review `snapback/generated/security-invariants.q.draft`: each role gets a row
question and a question for every column, including roles absent from the rule.
No candidate is accepted automatically. Use table-read intent for a shared row audience; add
column-read intent for distinct privacy boundaries. Assess every role and field
without automatically creating one assertion per question. For each role, identify
a positive grant from the spec or a necessary described workflow before retaining
access to another person's private data. Confirm intentionally shared fields
explicitly; an omitted denial is not a product grant.
Run check, test and audit again after confirming intent;
a refutation means fix the enforcement or the independently verified spec,
without widening intent just to make the result green.

Test synthesizes two users per discovered role/seat and related ownership rows
without fixture files, including password-only apps. These private in-memory
fixtures never open dev doors. Every failure labels its initial fixture origin:
`generated-fixture-trace` means an authored observation from a validated generated
initial state, not proof that app operations created that state. Author-supplied
fixtures retain `authored-fixture-trace`. Both fail acceptance on actual observed
violations; seed-only/synthetic table probes remain non-failing supplemental evidence.
Fixture-free tests report that no authored business cases ran. Finite passing
campaigns never prove security. Check bounds concrete exploration to 512 call/world
reservations and 1500 ms; exhaustion stays unproved and points to the full test.
`audit --json` retains all column audiences, grant writers, exclusions and uncovered
role/row/effect exposures. Audit returns the permission map with bounded check
evidence; run `test` for the full generated campaign. `audit --all` shows every
reason, without increasing the search. Declaring one effect does not cover the others.

## Reference

`invariant table.column read only P` protects a column value, derived values and value-dependent counts/existence/refusals. Mutation results, implicit final writes and explicit returned row objects are observers too. A query projection alone is not privacy: use column read rules to protect every operation. An authored projection excluding the column does not by itself disclose that value; filtering, ordering or copying it can. Audit JSON includes `column_audiences` (effective row rule plus private-owner gate). Unknown derived and opaque paths remain unproved.

`invariant table.read only P` protects read intent; insert, update and delete
name authority. `invariant table.column write only P` covers writes to a
column (insert, inserted insert-if-absent, update and both upsert arms).
A null creation or the declared `false` approval default is initialization, not an approval write. Mandatory creation presence needs whole-row insert/write or always intent; non-null/non-default creation still requires P. Tests judge each executed write in a committed mutation, including intermediate writes. `invariant table.column update only P` checks
only edits, retaining old-and-next-image semantics; use it when creation is permitted
to a different role (for example counter staff creating a ticket price). `invariant table always P` states row integrity.
An update policy over `.field` constrains **both old and next images**.
A policy saying status must not be override therefore also restricts editing
a row that was already override, even when changing it away from that status.
For the role authority of one named mutation, use operation-specific intent
(`invariant table.operation only P`) instead. An operator `provision` result excludes only its own executed roster write from authored read intent; later queries and other results remain checked. General next-image-only and
cross-operation transition assertions still need a separate reduction.
Predicates use the existing rule vocabulary: `.field`, `viewer`, `exists`,
comparisons, `and`, `or`, `not`. For a role write use the complete roster probe,
for example `invariant members.role write only exists roles[viewer, role = 'owner']`
in a schema declaring those tables. A declaration never grants authority. Whole-row read intent also protects logical
existence: when occupancy totals are intentionally public but roster identities
are private, declare column read intent for those identities instead. Include
a service seat explicitly when the spec permits it; seats are ordinary checked
viewers, never implicitly exempt.

The implemented proof fragment exhaustively checks independent three-valued
rule predicates. A failed implication is unproved until an engine replay
establishes a counterexample. A schema-valid seeded snapshot is not a claim
that an application operation sequence reaches that state. Required-column
non-null assertions have a separate validation theorem. Read/count/sum/existence obligations are reported separately. A maintained aggregate with bare privacy keys can prove its contributor audience; a broader aggregate can yield a paired present/absent counterexample. Exact server `not before`/`expire at` bounds can prove a temporal read component independently of physical sweeps. General transitions and unsupported expressions remain unproved.
Read-through captures/live parents, complex derived aggregates, raw rule probes,
owned-field update bypasses, cascade/set-null and opaque jobs/workers need
separate reductions. An unresolved path blocks the whole declaration's proof.

Server delivery authorizes against the current server snapshot. A disconnected
device can retain an earlier snapshot until it receives a revocation; even
`until-revoked` cannot know a later server role. Synced current-role intent is

# LLP 3000.036.004.000: Structural write authority

**Type:** RFC
**Status:** Implemented
**Systems:** Snapback 4 language, store, security checker and deployment
**Author:** Charlie Cheever / Codex
**Date:** 2026-10-23
**Revised:** 2026-10-24 (§9: Integration 24 wave-26 precision)
**Related:** LLP 3000.036.004, LLP 3000.036, LLP 3000.015

## 1. Scope

SEC9 implements the wave-25 bakeoff §8c shapes. Finite adversarial testing is
evidence, never a guarantee of arbitrary application security. Specification
questions remain explicit; neither names nor generated histories prove intent.

## 2. Column write authority

`c: T write <- P` conjoins P with ordinary row authority for every executed
insert with a non-null effective c, and every update changing c (including
null-to-value and value-to-null). Defaults count. Sequence-dependent grants wait for the assigned ordinal;
sequence-independent early admission may not suppress a completed-image grant. Upsert and insert-if-absent
use the same engine boundary. Equal-value updates require no column grant. Updates touching guarded or frozen
payload require readable current row/cell; hidden values never select an equality
bypass or a conditional-freeze refusal. Blind insert needs no prior payload.
P uses the ordinary read-rule grammar and current row fields. An update judges
P against both old and next images, preventing authority transfer in the patch.
System writes retain the existing trusted rule bypass. Referential actions
must not silently bypass a column guard: reject guarded set-null targets.
Freeze comparisons materialize missing optional fields as null on both images.
Older engines/devices refuse the new schema capability. Device engines
queue guarded writes before target lookup and retain the same raw-schema
validation and fail-closed rule/constraint evaluator. Serialized source reservation
plans mark guarded tables server-only. Prediction may queue
guarded writes rather than guess missing policy inputs. No budget is raised.

## 3. Conditional immutability

`immutable c when P` freezes c when P holds on the old image. Clearing approval
and replacing evidence in one patch cannot bypass it. Inserts are unaffected;
equal-value writes pass. This is a constraint, including trusted system writes,
not a grant. The condition may follow ordinary live write-rule reference hops
(e.g. `.tour.status != 'booked'`), but never named old/next operands or the
clock. Ordered current-row comparisons remain outside this initial grammar. Trusted persistence restoration remains the explicit raw-image
exception, as for existing immutable constraints; ordinary seed/system mutations
and imports enforce freezing on replacement. P uses current-row/read-rule grammar; unknown is not permission to
edit. Referential set-null cannot target a conditionally immutable column.

## 4. Asymmetry and presentation

A column-specific edit guard (including `old.c = next.c or P`) paired with an
argument-supplied create path not implying P demands individual review.
Explicit `invariant T.c update only P` acknowledges deliberately different
creation authority only when its policy matches the guard. A column write
rule provides enforcement instead. An abstract failed implication is a question,
never an exploit proof. Row-local old/next audience operands normalize to the creation image; known
creator viewer/literal/default assignments substitute into both admission and
audience before implication, so self-attribution does not create a false question.
The extractor covers unchanged-column conjunctions in an alternative arm.
Exact structural implication must first eliminate
conjunct/disjunct containment; inconsistent Boolean valuations must not cause
the dispatcher-subset false question.

Authorization and write-authority notes are never cut while lookup hygiene is
shown. Insert write intent ranks 3; the five lookup hygiene classes rank 6.

Audience extraction normalizes old/next row-local operands to the creation image and reduces identical total row-local/viewer/literal operands in equality to true (inequality to false). Live-reference operands remain unknown-capable and are never folded. Unrelated unchanged-field constraints must not manufacture a wider creator audience.

## 5. Derived campaigns and production deployment

Declared column write audiences also generate insert/update adversarial contracts
under `$enforced/` sites. These verify the language enforcement itself and cannot
be acknowledged as inferred specification intent; null creation retains its
explicit exemption.

Derive candidate intent only from explicit column change guards and approval
operations inspecting payloads. Approval-name recognition selects candidates
only alongside actual inspected payload dependencies; unrelated media do not
receive an inferred freeze. These candidates are inferred questions, not claims
that the specification requires them. Preserve authored source, label derived rules separately, and bind
acknowledgments to the exact source/rule dependency. Run non-null creation,
approve/edit and opposing Boolean candidates under ordinary engine admission.
Derived insertion candidates exempt a null column and require a non-null
witness to refute the change audience. Update/removal candidates do not exempt
null. Each derived identity includes a SHA-256 of its exact policy.
Unconditionally immutable columns and creator defaults/constants without a caller-supplied value do not imply a creation-audience candidate. Creation authority candidates require a caller-controlled insert/upsert value. Distinct verified image samples exercise actual replacement rather than identical asset identity. Every focused optional payload proposal retains an explicit null alternative beside its non-null alternative before creator population caps, so prioritizing adversarial non-null creation cannot erase missing-evidence histories.
A derived approve/edit candidate compares the actual old and next approval images as one private paired predicate: an unapproved old image OR an unapproved next image permits the edit. It therefore detects replacement retaining approval, while an edit explicitly invalidating approval is not a FIX. This private replay mode is selected only for derived paired policies; authored and enforced column intent keep their existing two-image semantics. The exact paired predicate participates in the SHA-256 identity. Authored conditional immutability remains the stricter old-image constraint.
Only a replayed reachable violation is FIX. Production deployment runs a bounded
campaign before release staging, schema adoption or activation (command/store ownership
setup and saved-source preparation may precede the campaign) and refuses unacknowledged FIX.
An explicit update-only invariant or individual rule-bound design decision may
dispose of derived intent; authored invariant refutations cannot be acknowledged.
Report time, attempt bounds and uncovered paths. All serialized schema semantics, programs and authored invariants participate
in release change detection, including lifetimes and reference actions.
No passing finite run proves
security; cached pass results grant no exemption.

## 6. Concrete intent and loosening

Aggregate element reference hops use the population element type, never the
enclosing invariant target type. Clock, duration arithmetic and cross-row references are evaluated against each
replayed step's actual clock and raw snapshot. Read-through child existence
uses logical lifetime, including parent lifetime, rather than physical rows.
Missing references, unsupported forms and exhausted search remain unknown.
Clock expansion prioritizes operations with clock-dependent Require/Check conditions (including local aliases) or authored clock intent. Other operations keep the current history clock and explicitly record temporal payload-only histories as uncovered, preserving ordinary lifecycle depth under the shared attempt cap. Clock-advance steps never move backwards; include stored schedule boundaries
and source-duration offsets from stored Time values. Four samples retain current,
a furthest source-offset boundary and the earliest future stored boundaries.
Generated initialization postpones a consuming parent transition once when another pending creator needs that parent, allowing child setup before lifecycle state is consumed. Typed keyed reads and lowered lookup/field comparisons prioritize principal argument tuples from matching literal
role/relationship rows; all remaining values stay in the bounded proposal pool
and ordinary mutation guards determine actual admission. This is search ordering,
including under disjunction/negation, never an implication assumption.
Prioritize unresolved operation intent before general roster mutations. Two validated setup checkpoints share the same 4096 directed-attempt budget: an early checkpoint before dependent/setup creators consume root state, and a stricter predecessor immediately before an admitted non-roster dependency/child change makes a known current-image intent false→true for an actual fixture actor/row. Refused or unknown transitions cannot spend the latter. Each gets at most 1024 attempts (the second cumulative ceiling is 2048), leaving at least 2048 for the final generated fixture. These checkpoints order search only, establish no admission fact and never claim initialization reachable from empty state. Retain admitted Boolean projections across all initialization rounds. After one creator decision is admitted, prioritize not-yet-admitted Boolean projections across the remaining parent tuples before filling the second root slot. A refused opposite decision on an already-consumed parent cannot stand in for the opposite decision on a fresh parent. The 16-tuple/2048-call limits remain unchanged; failed or omitted proposals never prove safety. Every cut remains
explicit coverage, never proof.

After a successful check, retain only a local schema baseline in generated
output. Required-to-optional loosening demands an authored always/insert/write
invariant containing an explicit focal-column equality/inequality with null
(or its Boolean composition), or an
individual specification disposition at the first subsequent check. A fresh
checkout with no prior baseline must not invent a migration. Deleting local
generated state is outside the baseline guarantee; release comparison must
independently enforce the same review for production loosening.

## 7. Validation

Use exact invariant-site witnesses, every guarded write arm and outside role,
hidden-target/meter controls and packed runnable guide recipes. Measure all new
gates and deploy refusals on the 450-project corpus, inspecting every new result.
Record inherited failures separately. No Mac execution is claimed in this lane.

## 8. Review record

The final semantic revision `699e0625a4` (including the main merge `b0faf93332`) received READY from two fresh independent
reviewers: Muse spark contributor high and Codex gpt-6.1-sol high, round 10.
Verbatim compressed logs, shared briefs and exact provenance live under
`llp/reviews/3000.036.004.000-sec9-r10-*`. Rounds 1–9 retain the concerns and
corrections that led here; no prior READY is represented as covering a later
semantic delta. Review readiness is separate from execution qualification and
from a human acceptance decision. The workstream report records affected checks,
full-suite failures, corpus results and omitted Mac execution.

## 9. Integration 24 wave-26 precision

Author instruction of 2026-10-24 identifies three false production refusals
revealed after builders run the advised test campaign. These clarifications
change finite intent observations, not application admission or the proof kernel.

Authored column `write only P` judges the inserted arm as `.c = null or P`.
For a Boolean column declaring `default false`, inserting that unapproved false
value is initialization too. This exemption applies only at insertion; every
actual changed update, including removal/null or true-to-false, still judges P
against both images. Meaningful defaults such as true are not exempt. Runtime
`write <- P` remains stricter: all non-null effective defaults count, as §2 says.
Concrete predicates and rule-fragment predicates use the same insert boundary. Consequently, column `write only` alone cannot imply mandatory creation presence: an upload gate needs required type, whole-row insert/write or always intent; those independent presence promises remain enforced.

For a mutation named `provision` (including a module-qualified name) run by
`service:operator`, authored read intent excludes only returned roster images
witnessed at that mutation's actual executed insert/update boundary. Existing
roster recognition requires a principal-keyed global role table. A marker in the
private fixture trace identifies the exact written image, and every typed occurrence of that returned row identity must equal that image. Returned preimages or mixed before/after payloads do not qualify; naming an operation
alone cannot exempt unrelated rows. Ordinary queries, other mutations, other
principals and non-roster results remain checked. This is an explicit trusted
provision-result scope exception, not a runtime read grant or a general theorem.

Approval-freeze proposals omit payload uses occurring only in `= null` absence
checks or comparisons of stage/status/phase/state discriminators to literal
states. Null comparisons are interpreted in their Boolean polarity: `not (.c = null)` remains positive evidence, while `not (.c != null)` is absence. Only plain direct row-field absence accesses are pruned; calls, arithmetic, conditional operands and complex lookup keys retain dependencies. Unknown expression contexts retain dependencies. Positive/non-null evidence uses remain inspected even when another use
is an absence precondition. Existing enum/Boolean/reference exclusions remain.
These are candidate-selection heuristics; absence of a candidate proves nothing.

One shared gate-code registry drives dispositions and human tiering. Every gate
must render as a review, independently of the performance-note fallback, and
presence loosening has an explicit urgent priority. The semantic analyzer revision is `sec9-2`; prior analyzer-bound dispositions require a fresh individual review. A structural regression
iterates the complete registry through the actual note renderer.

A clean finite generated/fixture campaign with no authored business cases exits
successfully and explicitly reports that no business cases ran. Existing cases
skipped because saved source selects incompatible working fixtures still refuse;
proof helpers requiring actual business cases retain their independent check.
Password-only engine cases can name explicit principals; that does not qualify
HTTP authentication or replace a real-login proof.

Integration validation adds wave 26 to the 600-project day-one corpus and tests
both fresh production deployment and deployment after `test`. Before/after
records distinguish real source/intent decisions, one-line specification repairs
and false refusals. The old lane review verdicts in §8 cover their named revision
only; Integration 24's amendment receives its own independent review artifacts
and execution evidence before delivery.

Integration 24 review record: round 1 Muse was READY and fresh Codex found
returned-preimage and negated-null gaps; both were corrected and regressed.
Round 2 was READY in both families. The mandatory-upload consistency correction
then received round 3 review: Muse READY; fresh Codex identified a complex-null
operand dependency loss, corrected by restricting pruning to plain accesses.
Round 4's complete semantic delta received READY from both fresh families
(Muse spark contributor high via the wrapper's Meta fallback, and Codex
gpt-6.1-sol high). Exact captured-delta and brief hashes, verbatim outputs and
provenance are under `llp/reviews/3000.036.004.000-integ24-wave26-r4-*`.
Earlier readiness applies only to its captured revision. Semantic review does
not qualify the pending full-platform/corpus execution or accept policy on an
application author's behalf.
pub fn apply(report:&mut Report,campaign:&Campaign){
    report.campaign_coverage.extend(campaign.unsupported.iter().cloned());
    let rank=|c:&Counterexample|match c.reachability{Reachability::AuthoredFixtureTrace=>0,Reachability::GeneratedFixtureTrace=>1,_=>2};
    let committed=|c:&Counterexample|c.history.iter().filter(|s|s.result.get("state").and_then(serde_json::Value::as_str)==Some("sent")).count();
    for c in &campaign.violations {for v in &mut report.invariants {if v.invariant.site.to_string()!=c.invariant_site{continue;}
        if v.counterexample.as_ref().is_some_and(|old|rank(old)<rank(c)||rank(old)==rank(c)&&committed(old)<=committed(c)){continue;}
        v.status=Status::Refuted;v.counterexample=Some(c.clone());
        v.reasons.push("concrete policy-false snapshot admitted by the actual engine; fixture origin is explicit, not an empty-state reachability theorem".into());
    }}
}

fn arguments(args:&BTreeMap<String,ir::ArgType>,variant:usize)->Option<BTreeMap<String,Value>>{
    use ir::ArgType as A;
    fn one(k:&A,v:usize)->Option<Value>{Some(match k{
        A::Ref{table}=>id(table),A::Principal=>Value::Id(if v&1==0{OTHER}else{VIEWER}.into()),A::Id=>Value::Id("security-argument".into()),A::Cursor=>Value::Null,A::Optional(inner)=>if v&4==0{Value::Null}else{one(inner,v)?},
        A::Text{min,max,..}=>Value::String("z".repeat((*min).max(1).min(*max) as usize)),A::Enum{variants}=>Value::String(variants.get(v%variants.len())?.clone()),A::Int|A::Decimal{..}|A::Money{..}=>Value::Int(1),A::IntRange{min,max}=>Value::Int(1i64.clamp(*min,*max)),A::Time=>Value::Int(NOW+86_400_000),A::Bool=>Value::Bool(v&2==0),A::List{of:Some(inner),..}=>Value::Array(vec![one(inner,v)?]),A::ListRange{of:Some(inner),min,..}=>Value::Array((0..*min).map(|_|one(inner,v)).collect::<Option<_>>()?),A::List{..}=>Value::Array(Vec::new()),A::Json{..}=>Value::Null,A::Bytes{..}=>Value::Bytes(Vec::new()),A::Image{..}=>Value::String(if v&4==0{"$security:image"}else{"$security:image:alternate"}.into()),A::File{..}=>Value::String("$security:file".into()),_=>return None,
    })}
    args.iter().map(|(n,k)|one(k,variant).map(|v|(n.clone(),v))).collect()
}

fn materialize_arguments(engine:&mut Engine,args:&BTreeMap<String,ir::ArgType>,values:&BTreeMap<String,Value>,viewer:&str)->Result<BTreeMap<String,Value>,String>{
    fn one(e:&mut Engine,k:&ir::ArgType,v:&Value,viewer:&str)->Result<Value,String>{Ok(match (k,v){
        (ir::ArgType::Optional(inner),v) if *v!=Value::Null=>one(e,inner,v,viewer)?,
        (ir::ArgType::List{of:Some(inner),..}|ir::ArgType::ListRange{of:Some(inner),..},Value::Array(xs))=>Value::Array(xs.iter().map(|v|one(e,inner,v,viewer)).collect::<Result<_,_>>()?),
        (ir::ArgType::Image{..},Value::String(marker)) if matches!(marker.as_str(),"$security:image"|"$security:image:alternate")=>{let mut bytes=PNG;if marker.ends_with(":alternate"){bytes[19]=2;}e.store_asset(viewer,&bytes,NOW).map_err(|e|e.to_string())?.column_value()},
        (ir::ArgType::File{types,..},Value::String(marker)) if marker=="$security:file"=>assets::file(e,viewer,types)?,
        _=>v.clone(),
    })}
    values.iter().map(|(n,v)|one(engine,&args[n],v,viewer).map(|v|(n.clone(),v))).collect()
}

/// Fixture-free campaigns retain empty-start bootstraps and populated role worlds.
/// The two initial states share the existing 512-single/512-pair work envelope.
/// Every declared intent outside the concrete fragment stays visible even when
/// no generated row reaches its observer. This is coverage, never refutation.
pub fn evaluation_gaps(bound:&Bound)->Vec<String>{
    bound.invariants.iter().filter_map(|inv|{
        let mut reasons=BTreeSet::new();
        if inv.policy.is_none(){
            if let Some(syntax)=&inv.syntax{reasons.extend(super::concrete::limitations(syntax,&bound.schema,&inv.table));}
            else{reasons.insert("no concrete predicate syntax is available".into());}

        }
        (!reasons.is_empty()).then(||format!("{} at {}: {}",inv.target_label(),inv.site,reasons.into_iter().collect::<Vec<_>>().join("; ")))
    }).collect()
}

pub fn generated_campaign(bound:&Bound,campaign:&mut Campaign,matrix:bool){
    for (name,_) in bound.schema.visible_tables().filter(|(_,t)|t.ephemeral.is_some()){campaign.unsupported.insert(format!("ephemeral {name}: generated memory populations and ephemeral mutation histories remain uncovered; durable campaigns use isolated hub copies"));}
    campaign.history_limit=64;campaign.generated_fixture=false;
    if !campaign.reserve(1){return;}
    match engine_with(bound,&BTreeMap::new(),Vec::new()){
        Ok(empty)=>fixture_observers(bound,&empty,campaign),
        Err(why)=>{campaign.unsupported.insert(format!("empty initial campaign unavailable: {why}"));}
    }
    campaign.history_limit=448;campaign.generated_fixture=true;
    match generated_fixture(bound,campaign){
        Ok(fixture)=>if matrix{operations(bound,&fixture,campaign)}else{fixture_observers(bound,&fixture,campaign)},
        Err(why)=>{campaign.unsupported.insert(format!("generated fixture campaign unavailable: {why}"));}
    }
//! Positive campaign evidence only; never a cached security proof.
// @ref LLP 3000.036.004#137-wave-19-correction-admissible-starts-and-persistent-refutations — exact source/fixture/executable binding.
use super::*;
use sha2::{Digest,Sha256};
#[derive(serde::Serialize,serde::Deserialize)]
pub(super) struct Record{
    #[serde(default)] pub recorded_at:Option<i64>,
    #[serde(default)] pub last_completed_at:Option<i64>,
    #[serde(default)] pub run_violations:Option<usize>,
    #[serde(default)] pub directed_attempts:usize,#[serde(default)] pub directed_peak_worlds:usize,
    pub fingerprint:String,pub complete:bool,pub attempted:usize,pub sequences:usize,pub coverage_gaps:usize,pub violations:Vec<security::challenges::Counterexample>}
pub(super) fn selected_fixture(project:&Project,options:&Options)->std::path::PathBuf{
    let flag=if options.flags.contains_key("source"){"source"}else{"schema"};
    options.flags.get("fixture").map(std::path::PathBuf::from).unwrap_or_else(||{
        let saved=options.flags.get(flag).map(std::path::Path::new).map(|p|if p.is_dir(){p.join("fixtures")}else if p.file_name().is_some_and(|n|n=="schema.q"){p.parent().unwrap().join("fixtures")}else{p.with_file_name(format!("{}-fixtures",p.file_stem().unwrap_or_default().to_string_lossy()))});
        saved.filter(|p|p.is_dir()).unwrap_or_else(||project.root.join("fixtures"))
    })
}
pub(in crate::verbs) fn capture(sources:&[(String,String)],fixture:Option<&std::path::Path>)->Result<String,String>{
    let mut hash=Sha256::new();hash.update(b"SEC4-test-record-v2/full-default/NOW=1800000000000");
    for (name,text) in sources{hash.update((name.len()as u64).to_le_bytes());hash.update(name);hash.update((text.len()as u64).to_le_bytes());hash.update(text);}
    let binary=std::env::current_exe().and_then(std::fs::read).map_err(|e|e.to_string())?;hash.update(Sha256::digest(binary));
    if let Some(path)=fixture.filter(|p|p.is_dir()){
        let mut files=std::fs::read_dir(path).map_err(|e|e.to_string())?.collect::<Result<Vec<_>,_>>().map_err(|e|e.to_string())?;files.sort_by_key(|e|e.file_name());
        for f in files{
            // Follow the same regular-file symlinks as the fixture loader.
            if !std::fs::metadata(f.path()).map_err(|e|e.to_string())?.is_file(){continue;}
            let name=f.file_name().to_string_lossy().into_owned();let bytes=std::fs::read(f.path()).map_err(|e|e.to_string())?;
            hash.update((name.len()as u64).to_le_bytes());hash.update(name);hash.update((bytes.len()as u64).to_le_bytes());hash.update(bytes);
        }
    }Ok(format!("{:x}",hash.finalize()))
}
fn fingerprint(project:&Project,options:&Options)->Result<String,String>{
    let flag=if options.flags.contains_key("source"){"source"}else{"schema"};
    capture(&super::super::proof_source::sources(project,options,flag)?,Some(&selected_fixture(project,options)))
}
fn path(project:&Project)->std::path::PathBuf{project.root.join("snapback/generated/security-test.json")}
pub(in crate::verbs) fn remember(project:&Project,options:&Options,campaign:&security::challenges::Campaign,complete:bool,consumed:&str)->Result<(),String>{
    if fingerprint(project,options)?!=consumed{return Err("source or fixtures changed during test; evidence was not saved; rerun snapback4 test".into());}
    let previous=matching_key(project,consumed);
    let now=snapback4_core::now_ms();
    let mut record=Record{recorded_at:Some(now),last_completed_at:if complete{Some(now)}else{previous.as_ref().and_then(|r|r.last_completed_at)},run_violations:Some(campaign.violations.iter().filter(|c|c.reachability.is_reachable()).count()),fingerprint:consumed.into(),complete,directed_attempts:campaign.directed_attempts,directed_peak_worlds:campaign.directed_peak_worlds,attempted:campaign.attempted,sequences:campaign.sequences,coverage_gaps:campaign.unsupported.len(),violations:campaign.violations.iter().filter(|c|c.reachability.is_reachable()).cloned().collect()};
    let prior=previous.map(|r|r.violations).unwrap_or_default();
    let mut seen=std::collections::BTreeSet::new();
    record.violations=prior.into_iter().chain(record.violations).filter(|v|v.reachability.is_reachable()&&seen.insert(serde_json::to_string(&(&v.invariant_site,&v.viewer,&v.operation,&v.effect,&v.reachability)).unwrap())).collect();
    let path=path(project);std::fs::create_dir_all(path.parent().unwrap()).map_err(|e|e.to_string())?;
    let temp=path.with_extension(format!("{}.tmp",std::process::id()));
    let result=std::fs::write(&temp,serde_json::to_vec(&record).map_err(|e|e.to_string())?).and_then(|_|std::fs::rename(&temp,&path));
    if result.is_err(){let _=std::fs::remove_file(temp);}result.map_err(|e|e.to_string())
}
pub(super) fn matching(project:&Project,options:&Options,sources:&[(String,String)])->Option<Record>{
    matching_key(project,&capture(sources,Some(&selected_fixture(project,options))).ok()?)
}
pub(super) fn latest(project:&Project)->Option<Record>{
    serde_json::from_slice(&std::fs::read(path(project)).ok()?).ok()
}
fn matching_key(project:&Project,key:&str)->Option<Record>{
    let record=latest(project)?;
    (record.fingerprint==key).then_some(record)
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests{
 use super::*;
 #[test]
 fn a_witness_free_later_run_keeps_failures_and_does_not_promote_synthetic_evidence(){
  let path=std::env::temp_dir().join(format!("sb4-security-record-{}",std::process::id()));
  struct Scratch(std::path::PathBuf);impl Drop for Scratch{fn drop(&mut self){let _=std::fs::remove_dir_all(&self.0);}}
  let _scratch=Scratch(path.clone());std::fs::create_dir_all(path.join("snapback")).unwrap();
  let source="table docs:\n  owner: principal\n  read <- allow\n  online only\ninvariant docs.read only .owner = viewer\n";
  std::fs::write(path.join("snapback/schema.q"),source).unwrap();let project=Project{root:path};let options=Options{positionals:Vec::new(),flags:Default::default(),json:false};
  let bound=snapback4_lang::compile(&[("schema.q".into(),source.into())]);
  let mut engine=snapback4_core::Engine::memory(bound.schema.clone(),bound.programs.iter().map(|p|p.program.clone())).unwrap();
  engine.seed_at([("docs".into(),snapback4_core::Value::Row([("id".into(),snapback4_core::Value::Id("doc".into())),("owner".into(),snapback4_core::Value::Id("dev:owner".into()))].into_iter().collect()))],security::challenges::NOW).unwrap();
  let mut c=security::challenges::Campaign::default();security::challenges::fixture_observers(&bound,&engine,&mut c);assert!(!c.violations.is_empty());
  let mut synthetic=c.violations[0].clone();synthetic.reachability=security::challenges::Reachability::SyntheticOperation;synthetic.operation="synthetic-must-not-persist".into();c.violations.push(synthetic);
  let consumed=fingerprint(&project,&options).unwrap();
  remember(&project,&options,&c,true,&consumed).unwrap();let prior=matching(&project,&options,&[("schema.q".into(),source.into())]).unwrap();assert!(prior.violations.iter().all(|v|v.reachability.is_reachable()));
  remember(&project,&options,&Default::default(),false,&consumed).unwrap();let next=matching(&project,&options,&[("schema.q".into(),source.into())]).unwrap();assert!(!next.complete);assert_eq!(next.violations.len(),prior.violations.len());
  let other=vec![("schema.q".into(),source.replace("only .owner = viewer","only allow"))];let selected=snapback4_lang::compile(&other);
  assert!(!super::super::report(&project,&selected,&other,&options).refuted(),"evidence for current disk source must not refute an independently compiled selection");
  remember(&project,&options,&c,true,&consumed).unwrap();
  assert!(super::super::release_notice(&project,&selected,&other,&options).contains("run `snapback4 test"));
  assert!(super::super::release_notice(&project,&bound,&[("schema.q".into(),source.into())],&options).contains("matching test campaign recorded"));
 }
 #[test]
 fn review_labels_missing_stale_and_incomplete_evidence_and_strict_refuses_only_reachable_intent(){
  let path=std::env::temp_dir().join(format!("sb4-sec6-review-{}",std::process::id()));
  struct Scratch(std::path::PathBuf);impl Drop for Scratch{fn drop(&mut self){let _=std::fs::remove_dir_all(&self.0);}}
  let _scratch=Scratch(path.clone());std::fs::create_dir_all(path.join("snapback")).unwrap();
  let source="table docs:\n  owner: principal\n  read <- allow\n  online only\ninvariant docs.read only .owner = viewer\n";
  std::fs::write(path.join("snapback/schema.q"),source).unwrap();let project=Project{root:path};
  let sources=vec![("schema.q".into(),source.into())];let bound=snapback4_lang::compile(&sources);
  let options=Options{positionals:Vec::new(),flags:Default::default(),json:false};
  assert!(super::super::release_notice(&project,&bound,&sources,&options).contains("NEVER RECORDED"));
  let key=fingerprint(&project,&options).unwrap();
  remember(&project,&options,&Default::default(),true,&key).unwrap();
  let completed=matching(&project,&options,&sources).unwrap().last_completed_at;
  remember(&project,&options,&Default::default(),false,&key).unwrap();
  let later=matching(&project,&options,&sources).unwrap();assert_eq!(later.last_completed_at,completed);assert!(!later.complete);
  let notice=super::super::release_notice(&project,&bound,&sources,&options);assert!(notice.contains("incomplete")&&notice.contains("age "),"{notice}");
  let stale=vec![("schema.q".into(),format!("{source}-- edit\n"))];assert!(super::super::release_notice(&project,&bound,&stale,&options).contains("STALE"));
  let mut c=security::challenges::Campaign::default();
  let mut e=snapback4_core::Engine::memory(bound.schema.clone(),bound.programs.iter().map(|p|p.program.clone())).unwrap();
  e.seed_at([("docs".into(),snapback4_core::Value::Row([("id".into(),snapback4_core::Value::Id("doc".into())),("owner".into(),snapback4_core::Value::Id("dev:owner".into()))].into_iter().collect()))],security::challenges::NOW).unwrap();
  security::challenges::fixture_observers(&bound,&e,&mut c);assert!(!c.violations.is_empty());
  remember(&project,&options,&c,true,&key).unwrap();
  let mut strict=options.clone();strict.flags.insert("strict".into(),String::new());
  let summary=super::super::release_loop(&project,&bound,&sources,None,&strict).unwrap();let denied=super::super::enforce_release(summary.as_ref(),&strict).unwrap_err();assert!(denied.contains("E_SECURITY_REVIEW"));
  let mut staging=options.clone();staging.flags.insert("staging".into(),String::new());
