## 19. Wave-28 output and witness decisions (DX11)

Commissioned by Charlie Cheever, 2026-10-25. This amendment supersedes
§18's default questionnaire and exact-set family command contract. It does
not change SEC10's detector or gate semantics.

A passing ordinary `check` prints one verdict line and the existing bounded
language-enforcement summary. Only gates and reachable authored-invariant
refutations block. Advisory decisions and compiler notes require `check
--review` (also `--verbose` or JSON); the verdict counts them and names that
command. A pending gate always prints its individual command. JSON keeps all
sites, IDs, bindings, acknowledgments and evidence, regardless of display mode.

Decision identity excludes incidental locations and wall-clock campaign
results. A single canonical note set feeds JSON, display and acknowledgment.
Repeated `--ack-security ID` and comma-separated IDs with one `--reason` are
validated completely and committed atomically under the existing lock/source
race checks. An invalid member writes nothing. An audience family command
expands only to current advisory members; prior member records survive member
additions. Each new or changed member remains pending. Previously displayed
family aliases may acknowledge only their still-current original members;
never silently acknowledge a newly added member. Unknown IDs explain that
they are stale, from a different source selection, or not decision IDs and
point to `check --review --json`. Relevant semantic dependency changes still
invalidate individual acknowledgments; unrelated declarations and comments do
not. Version-1 records remain readable; witness-bearing decisions require a
new analyzer revision so old rationales cannot silently close new evidence.

A concrete campaign result associated with a question is shown as a witness:
operation, caller, arguments, admitted effect, old/new image and candidate
invariant. It remains evidence about an inferred specification question,
never an authored contradiction or proof. Unsupported or exhausted campaigns
say no witness was obtained, without inventing behavior. Acknowledgment
records retain the displayed witness and candidate, alongside the rationale
and semantic binding. Upsert wording explicitly says that its existing-row
arm **edits**. Derived evidence cannot be used to override a reachable authored
invariant refutation. No credentials enter decision records or output.

The adjoining developer-loop changes preserve the contracts of LLP
3000.000.000.001 and LLP 3000.015: refusal attribution and read-filter evidence
are developer-owned diagnostics, never new disclosure in public refusal
responses. Hooks expose `usePage.loading` and `usePage.why` as projections of
`page.read`, retaining its completeness/freshness evidence. A first-run check
hint makes the pinned React starter discoverable for projects that skipped
init, without modifying their files. Staging reports name persisted clock
offset; upload overload guidance names serialization/retry; proof docs say
text must be on screen, and verification recipes use `serve-check`. Expo's
starter offers a web file picker and native image picker.

Qualification: targeted CLI/client regressions, witnesses for wave-28 011 F1,
011 F2 and 019 F1, copies of all fifty schemas with baseline and SEC10 codes,
packed consumer snippets/starters, affected selector and reference check.
Darwin is unavailable in this Linux lane and is explicitly skipped. Finite
campaign replay is not a security proof or a promise of builder preference.
Two independent reviews (Muse and fresh Codex) are retained under llp/reviews.

**READY — design review only.** I found no material security, correctness, compatibility, or missing acceptance blocker in [§19](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-b28-dx11/llp/3000.036.004-security-invariants-and-exposure.rfc.md:11), read against §§17.3 and 18 and the existing review/campaign implementation.

Specific conclusions:

- Quiet default output preserves pending-gate commands, advisory counts, discoverability, and complete JSON evidence.
- Stable decision identity retains semantic dependency invalidation. Atomic batch validation and restrictions on historical family aliases prevent silently acknowledging added or changed members.
- Witnesses remain inferred specification evidence; they cannot override authored refutations or promote finite campaigns to proofs. The analyzer revision requirement prevents old rationales from automatically accepting the new evidence.
- Developer diagnostics retain the public disclosure boundary, and hook projections preserve the underlying read evidence.

Implementation acceptance should exercise stale aliases across additions and dependency changes, mixed valid/invalid batches with zero writes, campaign timing variation, witness retention, and version-1 record loading.

Implementation remains in progress; this verdict does not qualify its execution. No edits made.
